## M9 — WEATHER AND ENVIRONMENT

**Goal:** The world is ecologically reactive; environment and civilization influence each other. (P:M9, S§21–23, B§10)
**Depends on:** M8. **Split into 6 sub-phases.**

### M9.1 Weather state machine & visuals — ✅ DONE (2026-10-01) — phone check outstanding
- [x] **The weather** (`scripts/environment/weather_system.gd`, `session.weather`): one state for the whole box — **clear, cloudy, rain, heavy rain, storm, wind, snow, fog** — looked at anew every three game hours (on the calendar's hours: midnight, three, six …) and going on as a **Markov chain whose table depends on the season**. The dice are the world's seed and the number of the step: **the same world has the same weather** on every device however time is cut up, and nothing of the dice is saved. `hold(kind, until)` holds it (what the player's tools will do in M9.5, and tests); `release()` lets the chain go on.
- [x] **The climate** (`ClimateConfig`, `scripts/core/config_types/climate_config.gd` — the plan's `climate.gd` — and `data/configuration/weather_temperate.tres`, `Config.climate`): the four tables, temperatures, what each kind of weather adds, precipitation, cloud cover, fog and wind by kind, and the thresholds of the conditions. Over 200 years it rains or snows on **43–48 % of spring days, 29–34 % of summer days, 50–55 % of autumn days, 35–38 % of winter days** (the fields' made-up rain of M7.3 had 45 / 30 / 50 / 40); storms belong mostly to autumn, snow to winter, fog to autumn and winter.
- [x] **Temperature:** the season's mean (11 / 21 / 10 / −2 °C, going over smoothly from one season into the next — no jump at the turn of a season or a year) + the day's swing (warmest at 15:00) + **warm and cold air that comes and goes over days** (±3.5 °C; from the seed) + what the weather adds (clear +1 … storm −4; felt over an hour, not at once). `temperature_at(tile)`: colder higher up (0.35 °C a level) and by the water (1 °C). **At or below 1 °C what falls is snow**; snow in the warm is rain.
- [x] **Wind:** a direction that turns by up to 50° from look to look, and a strength by the kind of weather (fog still, storm 0.75–1).
- [x] **What fell is written down** day by day (rain 1, heavy rain 2.5, storm 3, snow 0.7 units an hour; two units make a rainy day), with each day's warmest and coldest; forty days are remembered.
- [x] **Conditions are found out, not rolled** (bible §10.1), at each day's end: **drought** (less than 2 units in seven days; not in winter; not before the weather has been watched for seven days), **heat wave** (two days whose warmest was at least 27.5 °C), **cold snap** (two days whose coldest was at most −8 °C). Over 200 years: a drought every three years, a heat wave every four, a cold snap once in ten to twenty. Each is an event when it begins (and is marked ended); a **storm** is an event too (once a day at most). The chain drought → crop failure → shortage is M9.6's.
- [x] **The fields' rain is the weather's** (`Farming.rain_source`): a rainy day gives the soil what M7.3's made-up rain gave it, a snowy day too; the fields' dry spell counts the weather's dry days. (Without weather — in tests of the fields alone — the made-up rain is still there.)
- [x] **The sky on screen** (`scripts/rendering/weather_fx.gd`, `scenes/world/weather_fx.tscn`, `WeatherFxConfig`): what is shown **eases** towards what the weather is (six seconds at Normal speed, faster when the world runs faster) — the sky changes, it does not switch.
  - **Rain and snow** as one MultiMesh whose drops are placed and moved in the vertex shader (`assets/shaders/rain.gdshader`): nothing per drop on the CPU, the same on Mobile and Compatibility. They fill a square around what the camera looks at (smaller from near, so as many drops in less room), wrap around in it as the view moves, **stay inside the box**, slant with the wind, fade in at the top and out at the bottom, and are dimmer at night. 350 / 750 / 1200 drops by graphics quality. Rain is streaks, snow is slow swaying flakes; rain does not turn white in mid-air — it stops, then it snows.
  - **Light:** under cloud the sun gives less (to 42 % under a closed sky) and greyer light, shadows pale, the light from everywhere and the backdrop grey a little, broken cloud throws more patches of shade and a closed sky none (`DayNight.under_weather`, a pure function).
  - **Fog:** depth fog that hides the same share of what is looked at from near and from far (half at its thickest), dark at night; rain and snow are a little hazy.
  - **Lightning** in a storm every 5–16 seconds: two quick flickers of white light, the **thunder** 0.4–2.2 seconds after. **With reduced motion there is no flash** (the thunder is still heard).
  - **Wind:** the trees sway 0.6 times as much in a calm and 3.2 times in a storm, the way the wind blows.
  - **Wet ground:** the earth darkens in the rain within seconds and dries slowly after it.
- [x] **Sound:** a rain loop whose loudness follows how hard it rains, the wind ambience up to 10 dB louder with the wind, thunder (`SoundSynth` makes placeholders for `rain` and `thunder`; files of those names replace them).
- [x] **HUD:** a third line under the clock and the date — **"Heavy rain · 7°"**. The debug overlay has a weather section (state, since when, temperature, wind, cover, rain today and in seven days, conditions; and what is shown).
- [x] Statistics keep the temperature too. **Save format 14** (was 13): the weather's state, wind, record and conditions. Migration 13 → 14 (an older world begins under a clear sky, now), real fixture `tests/fixtures/saves/v13_world.sav`, test.
- **Deviations:**
  - **Rain and snow are not particles** (neither GPU nor CPU): a MultiMesh animated in the shader does what both would, cheaper, and needs no fallback.
  - **`climate.gd` is `ClimateConfig`**, a config resource like the others; one climate (temperate) for now — the template's climate and "modulated by recent player interventions" come with the tools (M9.5).
  - **Rain does nothing to the water yet** (M9.3), people do not seek shelter (M9.6), there is no snow on the ground and no leaf fall (M9.2). In M9.1 the weather is seen, heard, recorded — and reaches the fields.
  - The made-up air masses (±3.5 °C over days) are not in the plan: without them a six-day season never has a run of hot or cold days.
  - Cloud shadows do not drift with the wind (changing their drift would make them jump).
- **Caught while building:**
  - **The weather counted days from tick 0, the calendar from midnight** (a world begins at six in the morning): rain was booked to the wrong day for a quarter of each day. Steps and days are the calendar's now.
  - Heat waves and cold snaps could never happen: with six-day seasons the mean moves too fast for three hot days in a row. (Hence the air masses, and two days.)
  - A test listened to a weather's own signal with a closure that held the weather: it leaked at exit.
  - My edit scripts twice left files half-edited when a pattern did not match; they are applied in two passes now (all patterns checked before anything is written).
- **Numbers:** ten game years of the untouched default world under the weather (`tests/soak/soak_m7.gd`): **passed** — 26 storms, 3 droughts, a heat wave, a cold snap and 9 dry spells went over it, and the settlement fared exactly as before (its fields lie in ground the river keeps moist: the weather's rain does not decide their harvest yet — that is M9.3 and M9.6). 0.455 ms a game minute (0.449 before the weather); the save is 25 kB (20 kB before: the record of forty days).
- **Verified:** `test_weather` (12): the climate file; **`test_weather_transitions`** (150 years: every row that came up a thousand times follows its table within 4.5 points; the seasons have tables of their own); the same world has the same weather (hour by hour or in leaps, and its record), changes only on the three-hour marks, a clock set back or far ahead; changes announced, a storm written down, held and let go; temperature (season, hour, no jumps over a year, an hour's blend, air masses, height and water); snow when cold; what fell is written down (by day, across midnight, forgotten after forty days); **`test_drought_detection`** (a week without rain, written down once, not ended by a shower, ended by a day of rain, not in winter, not in a young world); heat wave and cold snap; **`test_weather_effects`** (a clear day dries the soil, a rainy day wets it, snow counts, fog does not; the fields' dry spell from the weather); saved and going on the same after a load; the version-13 save. `test_weather_fx` (5, in the game): rain and snow in the box (amount, kind, where, wind, quality); the sky eases (and faster at speed), wet ground, rain → snow; the light under cloud, the pure rule, fog from near and far; lightning, thunder, by themselves in a storm, reduced motion; wind in the trees, sound, the HUD's line, the overlay. Suite: **1055 passing**, no engine errors. Seen on Mobile and Compatibility (`C:\tmp\m91`): clear, cloudy, rain, heavy rain, storm with a flash, fog, wind, snow, rain at night, heavy rain from close and over the whole box.
- **Phone (Note20):** installed on 2026-10-02 at the owner's word (save backed up first: `C:/tmp/m91/phone_before/files.tar`); not opened or checked by me. **Not measured: what 350 drops and fog cost on the phone.**

### M9.2 Seasons — ✅ DONE (2026-10-02) — phone check outstanding
- [x] **Where in the year it is** (`scripts/environment/seasons.gd`, `Seasons`): pure functions of the game tick — the place in the year (0 … 4), a value or a colour "by season" blended from the middle of one season to the middle of the next (**nothing jumps when a season turns**), days until winter. What the seasons do is in one config: `SeasonsConfig` (`scripts/core/config_types/seasons_config.gd`, `data/configuration/seasons_config.tres`, `Config.seasons`).
- [x] **What grows changes colour** (one set of shader uniforms, set when the year has moved — no mesh is rebuilt): leaves turn towards the season's colour (fresh green in spring, as drawn in summer, **orange and — for some trees — red in autumn**, grey-brown in winter), the grass of the ground too. Each vertex of a prop knows how much of a leaf it is (`Template.leaf`, carried in the second texture coordinate): a broadleaf crown is shed, bushes, grass and crops turn but stay, **conifers stay green**, trunks, rocks and huts are untouched.
- [x] **Trees stand bare:** the crowns of broadleaf trees thin from autumn on (30 % gone in mid-autumn, 85 % in mid-winter, back in spring) — pieces of the crown are left out in the shader, each tree its own.
- [x] **Leaf fall:** in autumn leaves drift down through the box (the rain's MultiMesh a second time: few, slow, orange; inside the box, with the wind). Not with reduced motion.
- [x] **Snow lies** (`WeatherSystem.snow_cover`, 0 … 1): a snowfall lays it (white in about fourteen hours), every degree above freezing melts it, rain takes it faster. On screen the ground whitens **in patches** that grow together (terrain shader), and snow lies on whatever faces up — roofs, crowns, rocks (prop shader). It eases in and out like the rest of the sky.
- [x] **The ground freezes, the shallows freeze over** (`WeatherSystem.frost`, `is_frozen()`, signal `frozen_changed`): frost goes into the ground with every hour below zero and out with every hour above; frozen from 0.6, thawed only below 0.3 (it does not flicker at the turn). While it is frozen, **water up to 0.3 deep is ice**: the pathfinder counts it as ground (`Pathfinder.set_frozen`, `is_ice`: walked at full speed instead of waded; deep water stays water), and the water shader shows the shallows pale and still. A world opened in winter knows it is frozen.
- [x] **The fields' year** (`test_season_crop_rules`): grain takes **14 days of good growth** now (was 8) — one harvest a year. Sown in spring and summer, **never in winter, never in frozen ground, and never when it could no longer ripen before winter** (`Farming.ripens_before_winter`: the days left, each counted at what its season lets grow). It ripens at the end of summer or in autumn. **Frost kills what is still growing** (`frost_killed`): the event **"A crop has frozen in the field"** (… "in the bitter cold" if a cold snap caused it; a shortage that follows says "after the frost took the crop"). Ripe grain stands and is reaped; dead crops are cleared; the plots wait for spring.
- [x] **The settlement lays in for winter** (`Settlement.winter_factor`, `SettlementConfig.winter_prepare_days` 6, `winter_food_factor` 2, `winter_wood_factor` 2): from six days before winter the job board wants more and more food and wood in store — twice as much by winter's first day — so foragers, hunters and woodcutters are called more strongly; in winter itself half way back (it lives off what it laid in). The season is the "forecast": the calendar, not a guess at the weather.
- [x] **The animals' year:** `SpeciesDef.mating_seasons` — **deer have their young in spring, rabbits in spring and summer**, none in autumn or winter (as many in a year as before: the rate is raised for the shorter time). `SpeciesDef.migrates` — **the deer move to other ground on the first day of autumn and again of spring**: at least twelve tiles away, by a way that keeps clear of the settlement. **Each animal walks there by a path of its own** (the people's pathfinder), sleeps on the way where it is, flees what frightens it and takes up the way again; whoever has not arrived by the season's fourth day lives there all the same; time nobody lived through ends the journey there. Event **"The deer have moved to other ground"** (a line in the chronicle, no toast). Journeys are saved.
- [x] **The cold is quiet:** below −2 °C no bird sings (full song from 8 °C), no cricket below 6 °C (`AmbientLife.set_warmth`).
- [x] **Save format 15** (was 14): the weather's snow, frost and frozen ground; the animals' journeys. Migration 14 → 15 (an older world has no snow lying and unfrozen ground; the weather lays it within the day if it is winter), real fixture `tests/fixtures/saves/v14_world.sav`, test.
- **Deviations:**
  - **The snow mask is by what has fallen and melted, not by temperature alone:** snow lies after a snowfall and goes in the thaw. A cold dry winter is brown and bare, not white.
  - **"Harvest in autumn" is not a rule but what comes of the others:** grain sown in spring is ripe at the end of summer or in autumn; what is ripe is reaped whenever it is ripe.
  - **"Planner reads season forecast"**: the job board reads the calendar (days until winter). A forecast of the weather itself would belong to M9.6 if it is wanted.
  - **Migration is within the box** (there is nowhere else): the herd changes its ground twice a year. If there is no way to other ground that keeps clear of the people, it stays where it is that season.
  - **No hut is needed against the cold yet**, and nobody is cold: that people react to the weather is M9.6.
- **Caught while building:**
  - **A herd that moved by having its "home" changed did not get there:** animals walk straight lines, and four of nine stood behind a cliff or the river. Hence the journey by paths.
  - **The first journeys led past the village:** the deer fled from the people, turned back, and set out again — for days. The way must keep clear of the settlement now, and a journey gives up after three days.
  - **Looking for other ground cost 90 ms a migration** (sixty candidate places, each asked whether people could walk there — a path search each). It showed as a test of the journal card failing after a jump of two years. Other ground is picked by what it is (green, not too far), and only up to six ways are searched.
  - **Winter thawed in an afternoon:** frost and snow went as fast as they came. Thaw and melt are slower than freezing and snowfall now (the ground is frozen for 40–60 % of winter, a tenth of spring, never in summer or autumn; snow lies on about a quarter of winter's hours on average — it is a temperate climate; config).
  - Tests of the fields and of shortage assumed two harvests a year; they set their own growing time now.
  - Autumn was neon on the Mobile renderer and snow blew out to pure white (no terraces to be seen): both toned down.
- **Numbers:** ten game years of the untouched default world (`tests/soak/soak_m7.gd`): **passed** — one harvest a year (46–59 grain at the end of summer; in two slow years in autumn), the stores never below 0.48 days of food (no shortage, nobody hungry or sick), the fire always lit, wood in store 32–37 at the end of autumn against 16–20 in summer (**the laying-in shows**), the herd moved six times, 5 deer, 9 rabbits and 2 foxes at the end. 0.443 ms a game minute (0.455 before); the save is 25 kB.
- **Verified:** `test_seasons` (7): where in the year (position, blends without a jump at the year's end, days until winter); snow lies and the ground freezes (by the hour; a clear cold day; thaws late, not at the first mild afternoon; rain takes snow faster; saved); ice carries (shallows walked like ground, deep water not; thaw; a world opened frozen); **`test_season_crop_rules`** (sown in spring; not in winter, not in frozen ground, not when it cannot ripen; frost kills the growing and spares the ripe; the event and its causes; reap, clear, wait); the settlement lays in for winter (the factor through the year; the job board's wants and priorities); the animals' year (young only in their seasons; three years of journeys: each over within two days with everyone there; saved; skipped time); a version-14 save. `test_seasons_in_game` (4): the colours and bareness through the year and between seasons, leaves in the air (and not with reduced motion); snow and ice on the materials, at once in a world just opened and easing afterwards; birds and crickets quiet in the cold; which vertices are leaves, and that the world's meshes carry it. Changed: `test_farming`, `test_shortage`, `test_animals`. **Full suite: 1066 passed, 0 failed** (1055 before). **Seen on the desktop on Mobile and Compatibility** (`C:/tmp/m92`): spring, summer, autumn (turned and thinning trees, leaves in the air), winter bare, winter under snow (roofs and crowns white), ice on the shallows, half-melted patches, a snowfall.
- **Phone (Note20):** installed on 2026-10-02 at the owner's word (save backed up first: `C:/tmp/m92/phone_before/files.tar`); not opened or checked by me. The world there migrates to 15 when opened. **Not measured: what the leaves and the seasonal shader lines cost on the phone.**

### M9.3 Hydrology v2 — ✅ DONE (2026-10-02) — phone check outstanding
- [x] **The river has a level** (`scripts/environment/hydrology.gd`, `session.hydrology`; `HydrologyConfig`, `data/configuration/hydrology_config.tres`, `Config.hydrology`): one number — how far the river's surface stands above or below where it was made. At every look at the sky (three game hours) it comes nearer to **where the weather lets it settle**: the rain of the last ten days against the average (no rain at all: 0.18 below; twice the average: 0.15 above), less in heat and wind (**evaporation**; none under ice, and under ice it moves half as fast). **Rain raises it at once** (it runs off the land: 0.0026 a unit), **melting snow too** (the thaw brings high water). It has bounds (−0.36 … +0.45): the springs do not run dry and the box is not filled — **the water cannot run away**.
- [x] **Level water is an equilibrium, not a flow** (the plan's "coarse update"): every tile joined to the riverbed whose ground lies under the surface holds water up to the surface. Set on the tiles when the level has moved 0.02, and only once after a long absence (not once for every look that was missed). Nothing depends on the frame rate.
  - **Low water:** the banks (0.16 under the water as made) lie dry from about −0.15: sand, walked on instead of waded — **drying riverbeds in drought**. The bed itself (0.56) never empties.
  - **High water and floods:** from +0.24 the river is over its banks and **spreads over the valley floor** — a third of the box under a few centimetres of water, the settlement with it (it can be waded; the land it covers is soaked). It runs off again when the level falls.
- [x] **Springs and the river's run:** three springs in the riverbed (where: from the world's seed) stand for what feeds the river; **the current follows the level** (`WaterSim.river_flow`): a quarter as strong at low water, more than twice at high water — what floats is carried accordingly. (M3.5's fixed river current is no longer a placeholder.)
- [x] **The fine side stays the water that moves** (`WaterSim`, real time): what the player pours or scoops flows as before, and **is the river's**: at the next look the river's level has what was added or taken (water in a box stays in the box). While water is flowing the level is not set over it (put off twice at most). `WaterSim.sync(tiles)` tells the moving water, the paths and what floats that tiles were set.
- [x] **Events with their causes** (`test_flood_event_causes`): **high water** ("After the storm the river is rising to the edge of its banks" — cause: the storm of the last two days, if there was one), **flood** when at least three settled tiles (huts, the fire, the fields) are under 4 cm of water ("The river has come over its banks: the settlement is under water" — cause: the high water; with "Show"), **low water** ("The river has fallen in the drought: its banks lie dry" — cause: the drought, if there is one). Each is marked ended when it is over. Storm → high water → flood, and drought → low water → failed crop, can be followed in the log.
- [x] **The fields feel the river and the heat** (`test_weather_effects`): the moisture the land holds by itself goes with the river (`Hydrology.groundwater`: two thirds beside a river 0.18 low, never less than 45 %, a little more beside a high one) — a crop that withers then is put down to the low river; and **soil dries by how warm the day was** (`Hydrology.drying`: as before at 20 °C, half again as fast at 30 °C, a third in the cold). Rain wets it as before.
- [x] **Erosion:** when high water comes, one time in four the river takes a tile of its sandy bank (it becomes bed, a level lower: the river widens there for good) — at most forty in a world's life. A change of the ground like any other (kept in the save), and a line in the chronicle ("The river has taken a piece of its bank").
- [x] **Frozen water** is M9.2's ice; here: no evaporation under it, and the level moves half as fast.
- [x] **People step back out of risen water:** someone standing where the water has become too deep to wade is put on the nearest firm ground (M4's rescue) — counted apart (`BehaviorSystem.waded_out`) from real faults (`rescues`, still a problem in the soak).
- [x] Debug overlay: the river's line (level, where it is settling, tiles, flow, groundwater, HIGH/LOW WATER, FLOOD, the books). **Save format 16** (was 15): the river's level and state. Migration 15 → 16 (an older world's river stands where it was made), real fixture `tests/fixtures/saves/v15_world.sav`, test.
- **Deviations:**
  - **Rain is not added tile by tile and flowed** — on a 64 × 64 box with terraces and no hollows that would cost a great deal and end as the same level river. Rain on land goes to the river's level (run-off) and to the fields' soil; **there are no puddles after rain** (the wet-ground darkening of M9.1 stands for them).
  - **"Coarse update for inactive chunks, fine step for active chunks":** the box is one piece and all of it is loaded, so the split is by kind of water instead — the river's level is coarse everywhere, moving water is fine everywhere.
  - **Soil absorption → moisture** is done for the fields (and for flooded land); moisture of all the land, grass and trees is M9.4's soil system.
  - **Lakes:** the river's ponds are part of the one body; a lake of its own would be a second body with its own level (the code keeps one).
  - **Erosion is by high water, not by measured flow** (level water has no flow to measure), and only of the bank.
  - Springs are not shown yet (they are places to discover, later).
  - A flood does no damage yet, and nobody moves house (M9.6).
- **Caught while building:**
  - **The valley's measures were not what I assumed** (a height level is 0.4, not 0.25): the first numbers bared the banks and flooded the floor at the wrong levels. The test of the river "as it was made" now checks the measures the config is made for.
  - **A long absence cost half a second:** the tiles were set at every one of the looks that had been missed (a test of the journal card caught it, as in M9.2). The level moves with every look; the tiles are set when the weather has caught up.
  - **Poured water did not come to rest level** (water stops flowing at small differences, and over thirty tiles they add up): after taking stock of what was poured the river is laid level.
  - **People stood in water that had risen over their heads' wading depth** and were "rescued" as a fault: it is what happens now when the river rises, and is counted apart.
  - A count named `count` in an event's text made "(4 times)" of four flooded tiles.
  - Tuning went three rounds: first the river was over its banks 7–25 % of the time, then never low at all. Dry weeks now pull it further down than wet ones raise it.
- **Numbers:** thirty years of weather on two worlds: **high water 2–4 times, low water 7–10 times (2–15 % of the hours, most of them in summer), a flood 0–2 times**; the level between −0.20 and +0.34; the tiles set about thirty times a game year. Ten game years of the default world with its people (`tests/soak/soak_m7.gd`): **passed** — 3 high waters, 2 floods, 5 low waters, 2 tiles of bank taken, people stepped out of rising water twice; the settlement fared as before (least food 0.48 days, nobody hungry or sick: its fields lie close to the river, where even a low river leaves enough in the ground — that a drought costs a harvest is M9.6's tuning). 0.456 ms a game minute (0.443 before); the save is 36 kB (25 kB: the river's chunks are kept now).
- **Verified:** `test_hydrology` (11): the river as it was made (bed, body, springs, the valley's measures, nothing touched); it follows the weather (two dry weeks bare the banks, the springs do not run dry, rain raises it at once, days of storm bring high water and stop at the bound, it goes back slowly, snowmelt, slower under ice); level water is an equilibrium (one surface, nothing lower beside it left dry, the moving water has nothing to do and knows the depths, every drop in the books, the flooded settlement can be waded, back to exactly the water there was); **`test_flood_event_causes`**; low water and what it does to the fields (the drought as cause, the failed crop put down to the river); **`test_weather_effects`**; the player's water is the river's; the current by the level; erosion; saved and loaded mid-flood; a version-15 save. Changed: `test_seasons` (ice), `test_weather` (the fields' evaporation), `test_people_persistence` (stepping out of water). **Full suite: 1077 passed, 0 failed** (1066 before). **Seen on the desktop on Mobile and Compatibility** (`C:/tmp/m93`): the river low (dry sand banks), falling, as made, high, over its banks, and the settlement in a flood.
- **Phone (Note20):** installed on 2026-10-02 at the owner's word (save backed up first: `C:/tmp/m93/phone_before/files.tar`); not opened or checked by me. Not measured there: setting the river's tiles (8 ms on the desktop for the river, 40 ms when the valley floor floods or drains — every three game hours at most).

### M9.4 Soil & vegetation — ✅ DONE (2026-10-02) — phone check outstanding
- [x] **The soil of all the land** (`scripts/environment/soil_system.gd`, `session.soil`; `VegetationConfig`, `data/configuration/vegetation_config.tres`, `Config.vegetation`):
  - **Moisture:** a rainy day wets the ground and a dry day dries it (the fields' numbers: +45 / −14, more on a hot day), and it goes back towards **what the land holds by itself** — its place by the river, as the world was made; kept apart from the tiles so that it is never lost — from below by a quarter of the difference a day, from above by half (it drains). Beside a low river the land holds less (M9.3's groundwater). Ground under water is soaked.
  - **Fertility:** land poorer than it is by itself recovers (one a day); **a flood that runs off leaves silt** (`Hydrology.drained` → +18, up to 60 above what the land is by itself), which fades by one in four days.
  - The fields' own soil — what a crop draws, what lying fallow gives — stays Farming's: tiles with a crop are left to it.
- [x] **Grass** (`scripts/environment/vegetation_system.gd`, `session.vegetation`): `ChunkData.vegetation` goes towards **what the soil lets grow** — as lush as the land was made where the soil is as moist and fertile as it was made, thinner where it is drier or poorer (a dry spell, a drought, a low river), lusher on silt, thinner in winter (70 %). **It thins twice as fast as it comes back** (8 % against 4 % of the way a day), and **bare ground greens from the grass beside it** (alone at a seventh of the speed). Tilled or burnt ground that nothing is grown on turns to grass again (8 % a day).
- [x] **It shows:** grass tiles are coloured by how much grows on them (`TerrainPalette.grass`: straw-pale where little does) — the uplands of the valley are paler than its floor now, and a drought turns the meadows pale. A chunk is drawn anew only when a tile of it is far from how it was drawn (six or seven chunks a game year).
- [x] **Trees seed and die:** a grown tree seeds a **sapling** now and then (0.4 % a day in spring and summer) on open, moist ground within three tiles, not among the huts, not where trees already crowd — a new tree prop that is "a felled tree with the first of its growth", so it grows up by the nodes' own rules (a small tree after a few days, grown after a game year, nothing to cut until then). **Trees in soil that has dried out die** (3 % a day below moisture 40; a line in the chronicle: "A tree has withered in the drought", several in two days are one line), **saplings die in a cold snap**. **The forest has bounds:** never more than 1.3 times the trees the world began with, and below half of them nothing dies of drought.
- [x] **Forest coverage** is a derived statistic (`forest_coverage()`: grown trees per tile of land); the hourly statistics keep **trees** and **grass** now. Debug overlay: a "land" section.
- [x] **Wildfire hooks** (the fire system is M12 / M32): `VegetationSystem.fuel_at(tile)` (grass and tree, less the wetter the ground; nothing in rain, under snow or water), `can_burn(tile)`, `burn(tile)` (the grass gone, the tree gone, the ground ash — which feeds the soil and turns to grass again), signal `burned`. Tested; nothing lights a fire yet.
- [x] **Coarse and spread out:** every chunk has its turn once a game day, the sixteen chunks one after the other through the day (never the whole box in a frame; 0.3–2.1 ms a turn on the desktop), its plants right after its soil. After a long absence each chunk is brought up in one pass (ten days at most are made up). `ChunkData.mark_changed` for whole layers written at once.
- [x] **Save format 17** (was 16): the soil's and the plants' books (the layers themselves are the chunks'). Migration 16 → 17 (an older world's land is taken as it is; the trees it has are the measure of its forest), real fixture `tests/fixtures/saves/v16_world.sav`, test.
- **Deviations:**
  - **Grass does not take new ground** (dirt stays dirt): "spreads to adjacent suitable tiles" is how bare grassland greens again from its edges. The generated patches of bare earth are part of the valley's look; if they are to close over the years, that is one rule more.
  - **"Cleared by building and farming":** farming tills its plots (M7.3); there is no building yet. A plot nobody tends is still Farming's as long as its stubble stands — the "abandoned land" rule is there and tested, and has work when plots can be given up.
  - **Trees die and are gone** (no dead trunk left standing), and nothing dies of age.
  - Animals do not yet eat the grass down or starve on thin grass (their numbers go by the land's capacity as before).
  - All sixteen chunks are kept in the save now (their soil differs from the generator's): the save grew from 36 to 43 kB.
- **Caught while building:**
  - **A chunk was drawn anew nine times a game day** because single tiles kept crossing the line at which a tuft of grass stands. Tufts change with the chunk now, when the grass has changed enough to see.
  - **Soil sat 41 % wetter than the land holds** over the years (rain pushed it up further than dry days pulled it down): it drains by half a day now, and the bounds test holds it within 30 %.
  - **The drought could not be seen:** the first tint was too faint, and the land as made was tinted everywhere. Grass is fully green from a cover of 90 now and straw-pale where little grows.
  - The second round of screenshots opened the first round's saved world (burnt ground in the "as made" picture): the screenshot worlds are thrown away between rounds.
  - "The middle of summer" in my tests was half a day past it (the grass thinned by one).
- **Numbers:** thirty years of weather over two untouched worlds: **grass cover between 22 % and 42 %** (39 % and 36 % as made), **trees 244–322 (248 as made; cap 322) and 343–389 (372; cap 484)**, 33 and 121 died of drought, 180–222 chunk redraws. Ten game years of the default world with its people (`tests/soak/soak_m7.gd`): **passed** — 45 saplings seeded, 21 trees withered, 267 trees at the end (248 at first), silt on 1,435 tiles after two floods, 79 redraws; the settlement fared as before. 0.475 ms a game minute (0.456 before).

### M9.5 Player environment tools — ✅ DONE (2026-10-02) — phone check outstanding
- [x] **RAIN** (`scripts/interaction/tools/rain_tool.gd`): rest a finger on the land and **a cloud forms over it and rains** for as long as the finger stays; move the finger and the cloud follows (the view stays). 0.6 units of rain a second (an hour of the weather's rain is one), in pulses of half a second, on the ground within three tiles: **the soil takes it** (+9 moisture a unit — the fields too: ten seconds bring a wilting crop back), and **the river rises** with what falls on it (0.002 a unit) and a third of that for what falls on land and runs off — rain without end is a flood. One act in the history when the finger lifts: **Gentle up to 6 units (ten seconds), Moderate beyond**.
  - **Stimulus `RAIN_FROM_CLEAR_SKY`** when the cloud forms under a clear sky (uncanny: anomalous, large, weather-like) — **`RAIN_FELL`** (ordinary, faint) under a sky that was grey anyway; once more, stronger, when a great deal has fallen.
  - Seen and heard: a small low-poly cloud that grows in a third of a second and darkens as it rains on, its shadow on the ground, rain streaks in the column under it (the weather's rain shader, 150 drops), rings where it falls on water, the rain loop.
- [x] **WIND** (`wind_tool.gd`): **a swipe is a gust** from where the finger set out, the way it went — the faster, the harder (0.35 … 1). **Light things in its way are blown along** (ten tiles far, 2.2 to either side; a pebble or a fruit at up to 6 tiles a second, a rock of 14 kg or a pile not at all), **the trees bend** to it for about two seconds and falling rain and leaves are carried along (`WeatherFx.gust`), dust rises along its way (rings on water), **animals near it take fright**, a rush of air is heard (`gust`, a new placeholder sound). Moderate. **Stimulus `SOURCELESS_WIND`** (anomalous, weather-like — ordinary while the weather's own wind blows hard). With this tool a one-finger drag is a gust, not a pan (two fingers still move the view).
- [x] **WATER** (`water_tool.gd`, no longer a prototype): tap water to scoop, tap land to pour, as before (the button's rim shows how full the bucket is) — and **rest a finger, then draw it across the land: a shallow channel is carved** along its way, a tile at a time (one height level below the ground as it was made, no deeper; open ground only — not rock, not where anything stands; 24 tiles a stroke at most). **Where a channel meets the river, the river follows it** (M9.3's level water: the channel is as deep as the banks, 0.16, and can be waded). Moderate, one act a stroke; **stimulus `GROUND_CARVED`**. A change of the ground like any other (kept in the save, the paths know it).
- [x] **Ripples:** a rested finger drawn through water with the water tool leaves a trail of rings; so does rain falling on water, and a gust crossing it.
- [x] **OBSERVE trail:** rest a finger, then draw it across the land: the card tells of each tile it passes (one card, its lines changing; a faint ring marks the tile). Nothing is touched.
- [x] In all four: **a quick drag is still a look around** — only a finger that has rested (the hand's 0.2 s) rains, carves or trails. Lifting a rested finger that never moved is still a tap.
- [x] **Tool reveal** (`scripts/interaction/tool_reveals.gd`, `session.powers`): **RAIN shows itself after the first rain** (or when a crop first stands dry: looked for once a game hour), **WIND after the first storm, WATER after the water has been touched three times**. The tool appears in the bar — which makes room: with many tools the buttons shrink (never below a finger's size) rather than run under the Home button — **its button glows for six seconds**, a notice says what it does ("A new power: Rain — hold a finger on the land"), a sound and a pulse. Debug builds have every tool at once (and the call prototype); what has shown itself is kept all the same.

  - **Rain's amount is Gentle → Moderate by how much fell in one go**, decided when the finger lifts (the plan's "by duration"); the stimulus is given when the cloud forms, so people react while it rains, not afterwards.

  - **Two tools shown in one frame pushed the tool bar off the screen** (buttons on their way out still took room in the row), and six tools would have run under the Home button on a narrow screen. Buttons leave at once; the row fits itself to the screen.
  - **The first cloud was a slab** six tiles wide that hid what it rained on: it is small, soft and higher now.

### M9.6 Civilization reactions — ✅ DONE (2026-10-02) — phone check outstanding
- [x] **Shelter** (`scripts/people/ai/exposure.gd`, `ExposureConfig`, `data/configuration/exposure_config.tres`, `Config.exposure`): the weather drives people indoors — **rain 0.4, heavy rain 0.7, a storm 1, snow 0.4; the cold too** (from 0 °C down, up to 0.8 at −12). The pull adds to "go home" (0.45 at full), takes half of its worth from work and three quarters from walks, play and talk; meals and drink are untouched — and a pressing need still wins (nobody is held indoors until they are parched). **Going home in bad weather is taking shelter:** 40–90 minutes indoors (out of sight), and their day says so ("takes shelter from the storm", "… from the rain", "goes in out of the snow", "… out of the cold"). In a storm the village empties: five or six of eight indoors within two hours.
- [x] **Heat slows work** (above 26 °C, by 40 % at 34): less gets done in the same time (`WorkStep`), the strokes come further apart.
- [x] **Cold → illness:** someone who is not warm — warm is indoors (unless the fire is out in a deep cold below −8 °C) or within three and a half tiles of the burning fire — gets cold below −5 °C; seven hours of it and they are **ill with the cold** (health falls as with hunger, to the same floor, "Ill with the cold" on their card); warm again, it goes out of them twice as fast, and they get well. Events: "{name} has fallen ill in the bitter cold" (cause: the cold snap) / "… in the cold, with the fire out" (cause: the fire gone out), "{name} is well again".
- [x] **Cold → firewood:** the fire burns more below 5 °C (twice as much at −10; `Settlement.cold_factor`), and the job board asks for that much more wood, whatever the calendar says (the larger of this and M9.2's laying-in).
- [x] **Drought → crop failure → food shortage, causes recorded:** a low river leaves less in the ground (M9.3's groundwater, now 2.4 per unit of level, at least 40 %): about ten days without rain wither the crops; **a withered crop is put down to the dry spell, the low river and the drought**, a shortage after it to the failed crop — **drought → (low water) → crop failure → food shortage can be followed in the log** (`test_a_drought_costs_a_harvest`).
- [x] **Floods → damage:** with the water in the huts (M9.3's flood) — **the fire goes out** ("The flood has put the fire out") and cannot be lit while it stands; **food lying in it spoils** (4 % an hour) and **wood floats away** (3 %): one event for the flood ("The flood has got into the stores", what was lost noted on it), and a shortage after it is put down to it; **crops under water drown** ("A crop has drowned in the flood").
- [x] **Floods → relocation of homes to higher ground:** the settlement **remembers how high the water stood** (`Settlement.flood_level`) and which huts it stood in. Everyone flooded out goes to **a refuge** — the nearest dry ground above the fire — and sleeps there in the open. When the water has gone, **a hut a day is rebuilt on ground the water never reached** (the planner reads the flood's memory: above the highest water, within 16 tiles of the fire, reachable, clear of other buildings, people and stores) for 8 wood — the job board asks for it. "After the flood a hut has been rebuilt on higher ground". **The next flood does not reach them.** In the default valley the huts go up to the first terrace; the fire, the stores and the fields stay on the floor.
- [x] **People interpret natural weather too:** a storm breaking, the rain coming back after a drought and the river coming into the huts are stimuli (origin NATURE; not uncanny; large, weather-like; noticed forty tiles around). Most make of a storm what it is — the weather; **someone who already believes in a deity, and is of a spiritual turn, takes a good share of storms for the deity's doing (in the test, more than one in ten)** (the interpretation machinery of M5.3 as it is: what they believe and their nature against the "ordinary" of it). Memories and the day log have words for them ("was there when the great storm broke — …").
- [x] **Save format 19** (was 18): the settlement's flood memory and the huts waiting to be moved. Migration 18 → 19, real fixture `tests/fixtures/saves/v18_world.sav`, test.
- **Deviations:**
  - **A hut is rebuilt in one go** (it disappears where it stood and stands on the new site, for the wood): there is no building by people yet (that is the construction of a later milestone). Only huts move; **the fire, the stores and the fields stay** where they are (the fire is relit once the water is gone).
  - **Damage is to the stores, the crops and the fire**; a hut is not damaged as such — it is given up and rebuilt.
  - **Nobody drowns.** Someone standing in water that has risen past wading depth steps back out of it (M9.3).
  - **"Spiritual cultures may start attributing all rain to the Presence"**: individual believers may take a storm for the deity's doing; a whole culture coming to it is the beliefs spreading over years (M5's social weight), not a rule here. Everyday rain is not announced as a stimulus at all — only storms, the rain's return after a drought, and floods.
  - Heat makes work slower, not people thirstier.
- **Caught while building:**
  - **People stand at their hut's door, not on its tile** (a hut cannot be stood on): "indoors only where a hut stands" left everyone outside in a storm and made eight older tests fail (asleep at two in the morning: 0 of 8). Indoors is at the hut (or beside it), unless the hut is under water.
  - **The pull of shelter was too strong:** at first going home in a storm was worth so much that a sheltering person stayed in until parched (an old test's "nobody went thirsty" found it). Halved.
  - **Everyone fell ill every winter** (147 times in ten years) when the cold began at −1 °C: the cold that makes people ill is a bitter one now (−5 °C), and a warm hut is warm.
  - **A flood made 111 "stores flooded" events** (one an hour for each thing): one for the flood now, with the losses noted on it. And it took 12 % of the food an hour — two-day floods emptied the stores: 4 %.
  - **A hut was rebuilt on top of someone** (the 20-year soak: "Muthi stands where nobody can stand"): sites are clear of people and piles.
  - A hut whose people were inside held up the others' rebuilding: the first free one is taken.
  - The probes showed the drought could not wither a crop at all (the land beside even a low river held enough): the low river now takes more from the ground.
- **Numbers:** **twenty game years of the default world** (`tests/soak/soak_m7.gd -- --years=20`): **passed** — 48 storms, 4 droughts, 4 heat waves, a cold snap, 8 low waters, 4 high waters, **2 floods** (the fire out twice, the stores flooded twice, **3 huts rebuilt on higher ground** after the first), 9 failed crops, **one food shortage** (the stores below 0.4 days of food for 20 hours at the worst; rationing, foraging further; over), nobody ill with the cold, 8 people at the end, worst health 0.87; water and plants within bounds (the river's books balance; 277 trees of at most 322; grass 37 %). 0.536 ms a game minute (0.477 before: deciding with the weather and the cold costs about 12 %). The save is 48 kB.
- **Verified:** `test_weather_life` (9): what the weather asks of people (pull by weather and cold, reasons, worked out once a moment, heat and fire factors); bad weather sends people home (scores, decisions with the weather as reason, shelter indoors at the door, the day log, a starving person still eats, light rain); heat slows work; the cold gets into people (who is warm, chilly days, illness, the floor, getting well, a short chill, the events and their causes); the fire burns more in the cold (the job board, three winter days against three of summer); **a flood in the settlement** (fire, refuge, sleeping in the open, drowned crop, the stores, the events and their causes, nothing rebuilt while the water stands, rebuilt without and with wood, one a day, above the highest water, reachable, saved, the next flood does not reach them); **what nature does is noticed** (storm, the rain's return, not ordinary rain; the believer and the sceptic); **`test_a_drought_costs_a_harvest`**; a version-18 save. **Full suite: 1114 passed, 0 failed** (1105 before). **Seen on the desktop on Mobile and Compatibility** (`C:/tmp/m96`): a fine morning, the village in a storm (two or three left out of doors), a flood in the huts, the huts rebuilt on the terrace.
- **Phone (Note20):** installed on 2026-10-02 at the owner's word (save backed up first: `C:/tmp/m96/phone_before/files.tar`); not opened or checked by me.

### M9 — Completion summary
All six sub-phases are done (M9.1–M9.6): the weather and its sky; the seasons; the river's level, floods and low water; the soil and what grows on it; the player's powers over rain, wind and water; and the settlement's answers to all of it. **1114 automated tests** (1038 at the start of M9).
- **Automated tests named in the plan:** `test_weather_transitions` and `test_drought_detection` (`test_weather.gd`), `test_weather_effects` (`test_hydrology.gd`), `test_flood_event_causes` (`test_hydrology.gd`), `test_season_crop_rules` (`test_seasons.gd`), `test_vegetation_bounds` (`test_vegetation.gd`).
- **Manual:** "make rain over a dry field during drought → crops recover → people react" — automated in parts (`test_rain_saves_a_dry_field_and_raises_the_river`: a wilting field watered back to growing; rain out of a clear sky is an uncanny stimulus people react to); **not done by hand**. "Flood the village with tilt + rain → damage + relocation" — tilt is on hold with M8; **by rain (the player's or the weather's) it is automated** (`test_a_flood_in_the_settlement`, and the 20-year soak); not done by hand. "Watch a full year at Very Fast" — **not done**; the seasons were looked at in screenshots (M9.2).
- **Soak** ("20 game years headless: no runaway water volume, vegetation within bounds, invariants hold"): **passed** — see M9.6's numbers. The river's books balance and its level stayed within bounds for twenty years; grass between a fifth and a half of the land's cover, the forest between half and 1.3 times what it began with; every soak invariant held (nobody stuck or stranded, needs and health within bounds, the stores' books, the event log, the world saved and opened again the same).
- **Performance** ("rain particles + water sim + 20 people ≥ 30 FPS low-end"): **not measured on a device.** Desktop, headless: a game minute of the whole world 0.54 ms (0.44 at the end of M7) — the weather, the seasons, the river, the soil and plants and the reactions to them together cost about a fifth more.
- **Phone (Note20):** builds were installed at the owner's word after M9.1, M9.2, M9.3 and M9.5 (each with the save backed up first); **none of M9 has been looked at or measured on the phone by me.** The M9.6 build is not on it.
- **Save format:** 13 → 19 over the milestone (weather; snow and frost; the river; soil and plants; powers; flood memory), each step with a real fixture and a migration test.
- **Exit criterion** — *"The environment and civilization influence each other."* — **met, both ways.** The environment on the civilization: rain and the river decide the fields' water, frost and the calendar the harvest's year, droughts wither crops and storms send people indoors, cold makes them ill and the fire hungrier, floods put the fire out, spoil the stores, drown the crops — and move the huts. The civilization (and the player) on the environment: fields are tilled and grass grows back on abandoned ground, trees are felled and grow again, the player's rain wets the soil and raises the river (to a flood, if they insist), channels lead it, gusts move things. **Unlike at the end of M7, the untouched default world now has its dramas by itself** — in twenty years two floods, a hungry spell, droughts that cost crops, huts moved up the valley side.
- **Still to judge by hand:** how the seasons look on the phone (and what the leaves, the snow and the ice cost); the river at its levels (and the flood sheet); the rain cloud and holding to rain; hold-then-drag to carve; a gust's look and the one-finger drag it takes over; how often and how long people shelter (rain takes a little from work, a storm half); the cold's illness (rare now: none in twenty years); a hut vanishing and reappearing on the terrace; the toasts of the river and of new powers.
- **Not in M9 on purpose:** the player's tilt and shake (M8, on hold); people building, and anything burning (the fire hooks are there: M12 / M32); death (M10); a weather forecast; animals that graze the grass down.

### M9 — Tests & checks
**Automated:** `test_weather_transitions` (Markov respects probabilities over 10k steps within tolerance; seasons switch tables), `test_weather_effects` (rain raises moisture; evaporation lowers), `test_drought_detection`, `test_flood_event_causes`, `test_season_crop_rules`, `test_vegetation_bounds`.
**Manual:** make rain over a dry field during drought → crops recover → people react; flood the village with tilt + rain → damage + relocation; watch a full year at Very Fast.
**Soak:** 20 game years headless: no runaway water volume, vegetation within bounds, invariants hold.
**Performance:** rain particles + water sim + 20 people ≥ 30 FPS low-end.
**Exit criteria:** *The environment and civilization influence each other.*

## VS — VERTICAL SLICE GATE

**Goal:** Assemble the plan's **First Vertical Slice** and prove the core: take a player from *"What's this?"* to *"Oh wow, that person remembers what I did."* (P: First Vertical Slice, S§105–106, B§26.1–26.3)
**Depends on:** M0–M9.

### Slice content checklist (from the plan)
16–32 world tiles (use 32×32) · water · trees · rocks · one tiny settlement · 5–10 inhabitants · simple homes · food · basic needs · day/night · basic weather · touch interaction · moveable rock · water disturbance · tilt · one simple disaster (earthquake) · individual inspection · follow mode · simple memories · basic statistics · hamburger menu · save/load.

### VS.1 Hamburger menu v0 (subset of M14) — ✅ DONE (2026-10-03)
- [x] **The ☰ menu** (`scripts/ui/panels/main_menu.gd`, begun in M10.1: slides in from the left, as tall as what it lists, Back a page back) **now has every v0 section, in the bible's order:**
  - **WORLD → Weather** (new): the date, the sky now ("Now: Clear · 1°"), what is going on (a drought, a heat wave, a bitter cold, frozen ground), and the rain of the last seven days ("Rain fell on 3 of the last 7 days").
  - **PEOPLE** (M10.1, M11.2): Individuals (a tap goes to them: selected, the camera there), Families, Relationships, Important People.
  - **HISTORY** (M11.2–M11.3): the **Timeline** is the recent-events list — the latest first, a tap looks for the event (where it happened, or whom it concerned) — and Important People, Firsts.
  - **PLAYER** (M11.4): **Interaction History**, with its counts (for reflection, not score).
  - **SETTINGS** (new, always shown): **Audio** (sound on/off; all sound, the world around, effects, buttons: − 100% +, a tenth a step), **Haptics** (vibration on/off), **Motion** (only while motion controls are on: they are on hold, M8), **Save** ("Saved just now" / "Saved 3 min ago" / "The last save failed"; "The world saves itself every 2 minutes, and whenever you leave it."; **Save now**).
  - **Hidden until their systems exist:** CIVILIZATION, Map (M13.3), Statistics (VS.2), the rest of M14's tree.
- **Deviations:** the menu is built in code (`MainMenu`, like the other panels since M10.1), not `scenes/ui/hamburger_menu.tscn`; Continue / New World / Backup / Reset under Save are VS.3's. With five sections the first page scrolls on a phone held upright (Save is just below).

- [x] **The line** (`scripts/ui/widgets/sparkline.gd`, `Sparkline`): oldest left, now right with a dot; **counts and shares are drawn from nothing** (36 wood falling to 35 is a small step, not a cliff — the first screenshots showed exactly that), the weather's temperature between its own least and most (its days and nights).

A world saved before has no food-days line yet (zeros, as for any series a save did not keep): no change to the save format.
- **Deviations:** "Water" is the water in the world as a number (no unit yet: M15 can say it as rivers and lakes); "for reflection, not score" — no goals, no colours for good or bad.
- **Verified:** `test_statistics_panel` (4): `test_from_the_menu` (WORLD → Statistics; the numbers of the world now, in words), `test_lines_over_time` (three days of samples: people rising, food falling, the lines follow; "Over the last 2 days"; a new sample shows within a second), `test_days_of_food_and_the_stores_of_every_settlement` (the days of food; an older save without the series), `test_a_sparkline` (nothing to draw; level when unchanged; least at the bottom, most at the top; from nothing, a small change looks small). Changed: `test_the_sections_of_the_menu` (Statistics is there now). **Full suite: 1236 passed, 0 failed** (the band's day 0.719 ms a game minute). ### VS.3 Save UX v0 (subset of M22) — ✅ DONE (2026-10-03)
- [x] **No title screen:** the first launch makes a world and goes straight into it (saved at once); every later launch goes straight into the newest world (as since M1).
- [x] **☰ → Settings → Save** (the Save page of VS.1, grown): when it was last saved, **Save now**, and —
  - **Continue another world** (only when there is one): every other saved world, the newest first ("Seed 48213 · Year 3 · Spring · Day 4 · 2 h ago"); a tap saves this one and opens that one.
  - **New world** — asked first ("This one is saved and kept: you can come back to it under Continue another world").
  - **Backups**: the world as it was at its last saves ("Year 1 · Summer · Day 1 · 4 min ago"); going back to one — asked first — opens it and saves it at once, so **what happened since is kept as a backup** (it can be undone).
  - **Erase this world** (the plan's Reset) — asked first ("…for good, everyone in it and all its history… This cannot be undone"): its files are removed and a new world begins.
  - Every question has **Cancel**. A world is switched by reloading the scene with what to open next (`SaveManager.open_next`, `Main.switch_world`): this world is saved first (or erased), nothing else saves on the way out.
- [x] **Autosave every 2 minutes, on pause and on leaving** (since M1; `save_quiet_s` after the player changes the world); **backups rotate** (`backup_count` 2: world.sav → .bak1 → .bak2, the oldest dropping off).
- [x] **A corrupt save is brought back from a backup** (loading fell back through .tmp and the backups since M1) — **and now the player is told** ("The last save could not be read: the world was brought back from a backup"), and **the spoilt world.sav is set aside** (`world.sav.corrupt`) at the next save instead of being rotated into the backups in place of a good one (it was: the next save made the corrupt file .bak1 and pushed the good backup out).
- **Caught while building:** the notice of a restored world was cleared as soon as it was made (binding the notifications to the world clears them): it is told after. The confirm page said "Yes, go back" over "No, go back" (now **Cancel**); a backup's line was cut off ("Saved just n…"): menu lines wrap now, and say "just now", "4 min ago".
- **Deviations:** worlds have no names yet (their seed stands for them; names with M17); no copy of a world outside the game (export, cloud) — M22; the manual corrupted-file test is the automated one (a save torn in half), not one on the phone.
- **Verified:** `test_save_ux` (6): `test_first_launch_goes_straight_into_a_world`, `test_autosave_pause_and_rotating_backups` (the timer every 120 s and running; autosave and pause save; two backups, the newest first), **`test_a_corrupt_save_is_brought_back_from_a_backup`** (half the main save overwritten: the same world as at the last good save; the player told; at the next save the spoilt file set aside and every backup good), `test_a_new_world_and_back_to_the_first` (asked; Cancel; a new world, the first kept; continue it again), `test_going_back_to_a_backup` (the world as it was then; what happened since a backup), `test_erasing_this_world` (asked; gone for good; a new world). **Full suite: 1242 passed, 0 failed** (the band's day 0.715 ms a game minute). Desktop screenshots, Mobile and Compatibility (`C:/tmp/vs3/`): Save, Backups, the question, the menu and Individuals with wrapping lines.

Changed: `test_hints` and `test_person_card` (seven hints; the bible's longest line is 44 letters), the three opening tests of `test_main_flow` (a later launch). **Full suite: 1247 passed, 1 failed** — `test_person_card` counted every hint as the first four (fixed; 19/19 run again); the band's day 0.713 ms a game minute.
