### VS.5 Polish pass (Tier 1 FUN focus) — ✅ DONE (2026-10-04, after M15; skipped first at the owner's word)
- [x] Juice the five key moments: first touch reaction, rock drop, tilt slosh, rain from clear sky, earthquake. Sound + haptic + particles + reaction timing. — **three of the five**: tilt and earthquake are M8's, on hold (no earthquake exists).
- [x] Tune reaction variety & memory text; make "remembers what I did" visible: when a previously-touched person sees the player's rock/rain/touch again, the card and an emote show recognition; memory line references the earlier event ("It touched me again — like at the river last spring.").

**What was built:**
- [x] **Recognition** (`scripts/perception/recognition.gd`, `Recognition`): someone who lived or saw one of the player's acts (a memory going back to an intervention, theirs and not told), met by another of the player's acts, **knows it again** — the same kind first, else what mattered most to them; not within three game hours of the last (more of the same is not recognised each time); never nature's doing. It shows: **a new sign over their head** (`assets/ui/emotes/recognize.svg`: an eye and a spark, before the reaction proper), **a soft two-note chime** (`SoundSynth` "chime"), **"Remembers this · Praying — thinks a god reached down" on their card**, and **the memory goes back to the first**: *"Was touched by the hand of a god (2 times) — just as by the fire, earlier that day"*, *"saw rain fall out of a clear sky — a god's doing — and thought of a touch from an unseen hand by the fire, last spring"*. Where is put in their words when it happens (by the fire, by the water, among the trees, up among the rocks, out in the open), when as it stood then (earlier that day / that spring / last spring / 3 years before). Saved with the memory (`recalls`, only when there is one: no format change).
- [x] **Reaction variety:** how it felt the last time leans how it is met now — the glad more likely to wave, the frightened to run, the awed to pray (`Reactions._lean_on`).
- [x] **The first touch:** the touched person's body **gives under the finger and springs back** (`PersonView.poke`, half a second; less with reduced motion), warm motes rise from them; **the very first touch of anyone** (the TOUCH hint not yet done) has a wider ring, more motes, the chime and a strong pulse.
- [x] **The rock drop:** something heavy (a boulder) sends **a ring of dust out** and a second puff, a **lower thud under the thud**, a **strong pulse** when dropped from high, and **the view jolts** a little (`CameraRig.bump`; not with reduced motion).
- [x] **Rain from a clear sky:** the cloud gathers with **a far-off rumble**; **the first drops land with a plip and a pulse**; drops on dry ground show **small dark splashes** (on water, rings as before).
- **Deviations:** tilt slosh and the earthquake wait for M8 (motion is on hold). Reaction timing is unchanged: a touched person already reacts at once, and the recognition sign comes first.
- **Verified:** `test_recognition` (5): `test_someone_knows_a_touch_again` (the first touch: nothing known; hours later: the sign first, "Remembers this" on the card, one memory twice going back to the first; again at once: not recognised), `test_rain_brings_back_a_touch` (a season on, rain from a clear sky brings back the touch; nature's rain brings back nothing; saved; an older memory brings back nothing), `test_where_and_when_in_words`, `test_how_it_felt_leans_the_reaction`, `test_the_juice_of_the_key_moments` (the squash and back, the jolt and settle, the dust ring only for the heavy, the first touch, the chime). Changed: `test_reactions_in_game` (the first touch of all is felt strongly first). Changed too: `test_feedback_services` (a boulder dropped from high is felt strongly; set down, medium). **Full suite (alone): 1293 passed, 1 failed** — that landing test's old expectation; fixed and run again (26/26), no code changed since. Desktop screenshots (`C:/tmp/vs5/`): a woodcutter touched twice, the card and the memory.

### VS.6 Slice playtest (mini M26)
- [ ] 3–5 fresh players, no instructions; observe 10 min each; follow Appendix 4 protocol; record findings.
- [ ] Android: low + mid device, portrait + landscape, background/resume, kill process → world persisted.

### VS Exit criteria (GO / NO-GO)
- [ ] **GO** if: most testers touch a person within 60 s; most discover tilt within 5 min (with hint); at least half spontaneously comment on a person's reaction/memory; no crashes; world persists across kill/relaunch; ≥ 30 FPS low-end.
- [ ] **NO-GO →** iterate on M2–M9 feel (do **not** start M10). Record what failed and why.

---

## M10 — FAMILIES AND RELATIONSHIPS

**Goal:** Turn individual NPCs into a society; the player can follow a family and care what happens to its members. (P:M10, S§15–17, B§16)
**Depends on:** VS = GO.

### M10.1 Relationships — ✅ DONE (2026-10-02) — phone check outstanding
**Started before the VS gate, at the owner's word (2026-10-02): VS.1–VS.6 are still open** (see "VS — Vertical slice gate" above; M10's "Depends on: VS = GO" is waived for now).
- [x] **The store** (`scripts/people/relationship_store.gd`, `scripts/people/relationship.gd`, `session.relationships`, `ctx.relationships`; `RelationshipsConfig`, `data/configuration/relationships_config.tres`, `Config.relationships`): a sparse store of pairs — one record for the two, the same from either side — with **familiarity 0…1, affinity −1…+1, trust, respect, romance** (the beginning of a partnership, for M10.2), **kind flags** (ACQUAINTANCE, FRIEND, RIVAL, ENEMY kept on the record; PARENT, CHILD, PARTNER, SIBLING worked out from the people themselves, direction-aware), the last time they met and **a small ring of the notable moments they shared** (event ids). `between(a, b)` (the plan's `get`: `get` is taken by Godot's Object), `ensure`, **`modify(a, b, deltas, reason_event_id)`**, `kinds`, `family`, `of(person)`, `with_kind`, `to_dict/from_dict`. **At most 30 for anyone** (the weakest are forgotten first; family never).
  - **What the values make of a pair** follows them with some give (friends from affinity 0.45 knowing each other at least half well, no longer below 0.3; rivals from −0.35 to −0.2; enemies from −0.7 to −0.5). **The closer to an end of the scale, the less one more good (or bad) thing moves it.**
  - **Time** (once a game day): feelings cool back towards where they began (nothing for others, a little warmth for family), people who have not met for six days grow strange to each other, and a pair with nothing left is forgotten.
  - **A new band** knows each other: family (and one household) well and warmly (familiarity 0.9, affinity 0.35), everyone else a little (0.4).
- [x] **What comes of being together** (`scripts/people/ai/social_acts.gd`): in every conversation (M5's socialize) something comes of it, drawn by who they are, what they are to each other and the moment —
  - **Converse** (and the news on their mind is told, as before: M5.5's gossip — in every friendly act, not only talk). A talk brings those who **suit each other** closer and **wears on those who do not** (compatibility: how alike they are on seven of their nature's axes, with a little extra for the generous and peaceful).
  - **Help** (the other is at work; family above all): affinity and trust. **Gift** (food in hand, for someone one likes: it changes hands). **Teach** (to a child, or to someone who knows less of one's trade): their skill grows, respect. **Flirt** (two grown-ups, both free, not of one family, not of one sex): romance.
  - **Argue** (those unlike each other, under strain, who do not much like each other; less within family): affinity, trust, and stress for both. **Fight** (rare: a quarrel between people who already dislike each other, one of them aggressive, one time in four): more of it, and a little of their health.
  - **Events for notable changes:** "{name} and {other} have become friends", "… have fallen out" (… "after their fight"), "… cannot stand each other any more", "… have made it up (again)" (cause: their falling out), "… have come to blows" (cause: their falling out). Each is on the pair's record. Days: "lends Ama a hand", "is given something to eat by Jon", "learns from Ido", "quarrels with Ama", "comes to blows with Jon".
- [x] **Relationship-aware choices:** company is sought among the nearest few **by how much one likes them** (family half again as much) and **never a rival or an enemy** (unless nobody else is about); **friends now count among those whose beliefs rub off** (M5's interpretation, with partner and parents); help is offered to family most.
- [x] Debug: the AI inspector shows whom someone knows best ("knows 7: Ama +0.52 friend, …"); the debug overlay has a "people" line (pairs, friends, rivals, how often each act came about).
- [x] **Save format 20** (was 19): the relationships. Migration 19 → 20 (an older world is given what a new band has, when it is opened), real fixture `tests/fixtures/saves/v19_world.sav`, test. Broken records in a save are left out (a pair with itself, someone who is not there, the same pair twice, values that are not numbers), values outside their scale are brought into it, family ties on a record are dropped (they are the people's).
- **Deviations:**
  - **Social acts happen within a conversation** (the socialize activity of M5), not as activities of their own; helping is lending a hand during a talk with someone at work (it does not make their work go faster).
  - **Gifts are food in hand**; there are no possessions yet.
  - **Flirting raises romance and nothing more**: partnerships are M10.2.
  - **Strangers become acquaintances by talking**, not yet by working side by side.
  - Kinds EMPLOYER, EMPLOYEE, LEADER, FOLLOWER exist as flags for later; nothing sets them yet. SPOUSE likewise (M10.2).
  - Fights leave no injury beyond a little health (injuries are M10.2's health).
- **Caught while building:**
  - **Friendship ran away**: those one likes are sought out more, so they are liked more — close pairs stood at +0.98 after ten years, and nobody ever fell out. Diminishing returns at the ends of the scale, and talk that only warms those who suit each other, now give a spread (−0.23 … +0.65) with friends, cool pairs, quarrels and the odd falling-out.
  - **The news was told only in plain talk**: a lesson or a hand at work took the place of the gossip (an M5 test caught it). It is told in every friendly act now.
  - A variable named twice in the session's activation stopped the game compiling: the test runner refused to run (its guard of M7 held), and `git status` showed nothing else touched.
  - **A starved settlement no longer foraged** (an M7 test): people ate the scraps they still held (what was left of a meal, now also given) at breakfast, and the morning's gathering ended the shortage before anyone went to a bush. Whether that happens came down to who held what. The test now takes the food in hand as well as the stores, and **no food is given away during a shortage** (the stores ration it).
  - The soak cannot name a class that needs the configuration while it compiles (memory: headless `-s` scripts): the counts of acts live on the store.
- **Numbers:** ten game years of the default world (`tests/soak/soak_m7.gd`, before this write-up's final run): 28 pairs, 11 friendships (mostly within the two households), 2 fallings-out, both made up again, about 100 quarrels and no fight; 1,573 talks, 301 gifts, 239 lessons, 163 hands lent. Final run: 28 pairs, 11 friends, 0 rivals; 1,573 talks, 301 gifts, 239 lessons, 163 hands lent, 100 quarrels, no fight; hungriest anyone was 0.00, worst health 0.87; 0.539 ms a game minute; SOAK PASSED.
- **Verified:** `test_relationships` (9): **`test_relationship_creation`** (a new band, one record per pair, family from the people in either direction); **`test_relationship_changes`** (diminishing returns, friends with give, fallen out, enemies, made up with its cause, the events and their words, values within scale, cooling, growing strange, forgetting — never family); nobody keeps too many; **`test_malformed_relationship_repair`**; what comes of being together (compatibility, what may come of it when, each act's effect, the fight and its event, the day's words); people seek out those they like (not rivals, unless nobody else; friends' beliefs rub off); in the running world people get to know each other; saved; a version-19 save. Changed: `test_behavior` (whom a talk turns to after the partner has gone in), `test_shortage` (the evening's starvation takes the food in hand too). **Full suite: 1123 passed, 0 failed** (1114 before).
- **Phone (Note20):** installed on 2026-10-02 at the owner's word (save backed up first: `C:/tmp/m101/phone_before/files.tar`); not opened or checked by me. Nothing of M10.1 is visible yet except in the debug inspector (the family UI is M10.4).

### M10.2 Lifecycle: partnership, birth, aging, death — ✅ DONE (2026-10-02) — phone check outstanding
(Before the VS gate, at the owner's word: see M10.1.)
- [x] **The lifecycle** (`scripts/people/lifecycle_system.gd`, class `Lifecycle`, `session.lifecycle`, `ctx.lifecycle`; `LifeConfig`, `data/configuration/life_config.tres`, `Config.life`), once a game day (in play from the session's frame, in the soak from the behaviour's step):
  - **Growing up and old:** a new stage of life is a new kind of work when the old one is not open to it (a child's play becomes foraging, then adult work; an elder's). "… has come of age", "… is one of the elders now". Children already grew visibly (M4.2); **a baby (its first year) is smaller still**, and **keeps to its parent** (it only follows them, eats, drinks and sleeps until it is two).
  - **Partners:** two grown-ups, both free, of either sex to the other, **not close kin** (not parent or grandparent of the other, nor sharing a parent or grandparent: brothers and sisters, aunts and uncles, first cousins — the dead count too; they do not flirt either), at most 14 years apart, with romance ≥ 0.35 and affinity ≥ 0.15 become partners ("… have become partners", caused by their friendship if they were friends). Romance comes of flirting (M10.1) — and now also of **chemistry**: what draws a pair to each other beyond how alike they are (the same for the pair always); those drawn to each other flirt even before they like each other much, and gain more romance by it.
  - **Children:** a woman with a partner, an adult, up to 40, her last child at least 36 days (1.5 years) old, **a roof with room** (six under one roof) and **food in store for a day** and no shortage: 2% a day. She carries the child **18 days** (three quarters of a year); then it is born at her side: **its nature from both parents** (Traits.inherit), **its family name from the father** (config), **its given name new — or, one time in four, a forebear's** of its sex whose name nobody living bears (legacy). "{name} was born to {mother} and {father}" ("The first child born here: …"); "has a child: …" in their days; the parents remember it; the family knows it as family.
  - **Death:** each day a chance of dying — a little for everyone (0.4% a year), more for babies (+2% a year to age 2), **for old age from 52: 5% a year, doubling every 6 years**; all of it × (1 + 6 × (1 − health)²); and on top: **starvation** (weak with hunger, at the end of their strength, for 3 days: 6% a day), **illness** (gravely ill, or ill with the cold: 4% a day), **injury** (a wound worse than 0.6: up to 3% a day). What they died of is drawn by the share of each. "{name} has died, old and full of years / of an illness / of the cold / of the wounds of a fight / of a fall / has starved to death".
  - **Newcomers:** a band of two or three families runs out of partners. When a grown-up (up to 40) has nobody they could become partners with and there is room under the roofs, **someone from far away comes** (3% a day), of the other sex and about their age: "… has come from far away to live here" (introduced to everyone, and with a spark between them and the one they came for).
- [x] **Health** (`scripts/people/ai/health.gd`): **injuries** (kind, severity; a fight's (M10.1's fight now wounds instead of taking health), an accident at work: 0.4% a working day, a fall or a cut, 0.15 … 0.7) take health at once and give it back as they heal (8% of severity a day, **twice as fast resting** — asleep, indoors, at home); **illness** from **bad water** (a puddle or floodwater — water that is neither the river's nor there of old: 15% a drink; the player's rain makes puddles) and **crowding** (1% a day for each one too many under a roof) takes 6% health a day down to 0.15 and passes (20% a day, twice as often resting); then health comes back. **Someone hurt or ill goes home to rest** ("goes home to rest") and works and plays less. Herbs: there are none yet.
- [x] **Households** (`scripts/people/households.gd`, `session.households`): who lives together and their home (one store feeds everyone, so a household keeps no stock of its own yet). **A couple founds a household** under the roof with most room (or, if one of them lived alone, the other moves in; their children from before come along); **children left without a grown-up are taken in** by family — grandparents, grown brothers and sisters, aunts and uncles — or by whoever has most room ("…, left alone, has been taken in by …"); **an elder left alone moves in with a child**; a household nobody is left in is gone.
- [x] **Inheritance:** the home stays with the household; **what they carried goes to the stores**; **their three most important memories go to their children** (else their partner, else their brothers and sisters) as inherited memories: "remembers how Ama felt the touch of a spirit" (60% of the weight). A role: the settlement already makes sure someone farms and hunts (M7).
- [x] **The dead are kept** (`scripts/history/history_archive.gd`, `historical_person.gd`, `session.archive`; brought forward from M10.3, as far as names and family): name, sex, birth and death, cause, parents, children, partner, work, household, where they died. **Everything that names someone finds the dead too** (`PersonRegistry.name_of`): events, memories, days. Their relationships go with them. The debug "kill" is a death like any other (cause "debug": "… is gone").
- [x] Debug: the AI inspector's "life:" line (partner, children, household, with child, hurt, ill, the chance of dying today); the overlay's "people" section counts lives.
- [x] **Save format 21** (was 20): households, the lifecycle's day and counts, the archive. Migration 20 → 21 (an older world has nobody dead; its households are as its people have them), real fixture `tests/fixtures/saves/v20_world.sav`, test. Broken records are left out.
- **Deviations:**
  - **Births are capped by the roofs** (three huts × six): building new homes is the settlement planner's (M12); until then the population levels off at about 18.
  - **Newcomers** are not in the plan: without them a band of eight dies out (a closed band runs out of partners within a generation). They come only for someone who has nobody, and only while there is room.
  - **Babies toddle after their parent**; nobody carries them yet.
  - No separations, no partners of one sex having children, no "culture norms" for partnership (M19 brings culture). Partners at most 14 years apart is the only norm.
  - No disasters kill yet (the cause exists); no herbs; no shared household stock; no contagion beyond a crowded roof.
  - The archive holds what M10.2 needs (names, family, cause); graves, obituaries, mourning, accomplishments and top memories are M10.3.
  - **Deterministic continuation after save/load (the M10 soak's last check) is not done**: people's in-flight thinking and walking are not saved, so a loaded world goes on differently from the one that was saved. It needs its own piece of work (see "For you to judge").
- **Caught while building:**
  - **Relationships never settled in play** (M10.1): their daily cooling and forgetting ran only in the soak and tests. The session now settles them every frame (once a day does the work).
  - **Nobody became partners in 20 years** (first soak): the free grown-ups of a small band hardly ever liked each other enough to flirt, and two days of food in store — which the settlement rarely has — were asked for a child. Chemistry, one day of food, and newcomers.
  - **A baby walked off on its own** (screenshot): babies keep to their parent now.
  - **The long soaks found people standing in water too deep to stand in** (once in 40 years, twice in 100): the river rose under someone drinking at the shore, and they were only moved out once a walk of theirs was blocked (an M9.3 matter that only long runs show). Whoever finds the water too deep under them now steps out of it at their next turn. (On the way, a baby born at night beside its sleeping mother is now indoors with her, and the soak's message says what the tile is and what they were doing.)
  - **Few became partners in 100 years** (4, first 100-year soak): a newcomer and the one they came for hardly met. They now begin with a spark between them (familiarity, affinity, romance 0.2), and romance fades half as fast as other feelings (1.5% a day).
- **Numbers:** 100 game years of the default world (`tests/soak/soak_m7.gd -- --years=100`, seed 12345; run before the newcomer spark, slower romance and the step out of risen water): 8 people at the start, between 6 and 17, 11 at the end; 17 born, 9 newcomers, 4 couples formed, 23 died (17 of old age at 54 … 70; 6 of illness, one a baby, one a child of 9); **6 generations**; 3 shortages, nobody starved; 0.75 ms a game minute (0.54 with the band of M10.1). The last 100-year run on the final code (seed 12345) ended with **2 people** (a peak of 18; 5 couples, 32 died, 18 born, 8 newcomers; 119 fights) — it passed its checks, but the band nearly died out: courtship was added in M10.3, after which three seeds held over 100 years. 40 years earlier in the work: 8 → 18 (the roofs' limit) by year 12.

### M10 — Completion summary
All four sub-phases are done (M10.1–M10.4) — **before the VS gate, at the owner's word (VS.1–VS.6 still open; a first piece of VS.1's menu came with M10.4)**: what people are to each other, a life from birth to death, death that leaves history, and the family in the UI. **1153 automated tests** (1114 at the start of M10).
- **Automated tests named in the plan:** `test_relationship_creation`, `test_relationship_changes`, `test_malformed_relationship_repair` (`test_relationships.gd`); `test_birth`, `test_aging_stage_transitions`, `test_inheritance` (`test_lifecycle.gd`); `test_death_archives_person` (`test_lifecycle.gd` and `test_death_history.gd`); `test_family_tree_reconstruction` (`test_family_ui.gd`).
- **Soak** ("100 game years headless from 8 people: population doesn't explode/extinct, ≥ 4 generations, invariants pass, save/load at year 50 identical continuation"): **passed but for the last check** — three worlds (seeds 12345, 777, 4242) over 100 years: 6 … 19 people, never gone and never past the roofs, **6 generations** each, every invariant held; the world saved and opened again the same at the end. **Identical continuation after a save at year 50 is not done**: people's half-finished thinking and walking are not saved, so a loaded world goes on differently (two runs from the start are identical, number for number). One run before courtship (M10.3) came down to 2 people.
- **Manual** ("follow a family across 3 generations at Very Fast; view tree; visit a grave"): **not done by hand.** The tree (three generations, one dead) and a grave visited (a mourner kneeling) were looked at in desktop screenshots.
- **Phone (Note20):** builds were installed at the owner's word after M10.1, M10.2 and M10.3 (each with the save backed up first); **none of M10 has been looked at on the phone by me.** The M10.4 build is not on it.
- **Save format:** 19 → 22 over the milestone (relationships; households, lives, the archive; graves), each step with a real fixture and a migration test.
- **Exit criterion** — *"The player can follow a family and become interested in what happens to its members."* — **met as far as the game can show it without players**: people pair up (by liking, romance and chemistry), have children named after forebears, grow up into their parents' trades, are hurt and fall ill, grow old and die; the dead are buried where they can be read, mourned and visited; and the player can find any of them — the living on the map, the dead at their graves — from a person card, a grave or the menu's family trees. **Whether it makes a player care is for players to say (VS.6).**
- **Still to judge by hand:** how often people quarrel and fight (thousands of quarrels and up to 221 fights in 100 years); couples are still few in some worlds (5 in 100 years in one); newcomers (not in the plan) keep small bands alive; graves 6 … 12 tiles from the fire; babies toddle after their parent rather than being carried; the ☰ menu's look and slide on the phone.
- **Not in M10 on purpose:** separations, funerals and rites, the ANCESTOR interpretation near graves (M11/M19), new homes (M12: the roofs cap the band at about 18), a family's own stores.

### M10 — Tests & checks
**Automated:** `test_relationship_creation`, `test_relationship_changes` (positive interactions raise affinity; conflict lowers; reconciliation possible), `test_birth` (preconditions; traits inheritance within bounds), `test_aging_stage_transitions`, `test_death_archives_person`, `test_inheritance`, `test_family_tree_reconstruction` (incl. dead), `test_malformed_relationship_repair`.
**Soak:** 100 game years headless from 8 people: population doesn't explode/extinct under defaults (target band tunable), ≥ 4 generations, invariants pass, save/load at year 50 identical continuation (same seed, deterministic).
**Manual:** follow a family across 3 generations at Very Fast; view tree; visit a grave.
**Exit criteria:** *The player can follow a family and become interested in what happens to its members.*

## M11 — MEMORY AND HISTORY

**Goal:** Actions persist beyond the moment; the world begins generating stories naturally. (P:M11, S§18, S§61–62, S§64, B§15, B§21)
**Depends on:** M10.

### M11.1 Memory types expansion — ✅ DONE (2026-10-02) — phone check outstanding
(Before the VS gate, at the owner's word: VS.1–VS.6 are still open.)
- [x] **A life's own moments, remembered** (`Lifecycle.remember_life`, `Memory.KIND_LIFE`): a child born ("saw their child born"), someone lost ("lost Ama"), a partner found ("became Jon's partner"), coming of age, being hurt at work, falling ill, being taken in by family ("was taken in by family when Huto died"), coming from far away, **a flood lived through** (everyone of the settlement: "lived through the flood"). Templates in `data/text/memories.csv`. One's own life is not news to gossip, nor a bedtime story.
- [x] **What was lived strongly lasts** (`MemoryConfig.lasting_from` 0.6, `lasting_fade` 0.04): until now every memory faded at one pace, and **the untouched world of the 30-year soak ended with no memories at all** — a mother forgot her child's birth within a year and a half. Memories of what was lived strongly (intensity 0.6 or more: a child, a loss, a partner, a strong touch of the Presence) now fade 25 times more slowly — for decades; the rest as before.
- [x] **Family memories: bedtime stories** (`scripts/perception/stories.gd`, on the behaviour's `bedtime` hook of M5): when a child goes to bed, a parent or grandparent nearby (within 10 tiles, or under the same roof) tells them a story (80% of bedtimes) — the most important thing they remember (at least 0.15) that the child has not heard from them; grandparents' stories count a little more (bible: "elders tell grandchildren"). The child keeps it **as they make of it** (their own nature, convictions, family and what the settlement remembers reinterpret it: `Interpretation`), **a little less true** (fidelity × 0.85 each retelling), at 70% of its weight, from when it happened ("Age 8 · was told at bedtime by Gluladru of a touch from an unseen hand — a spirit's doing"); it moves their convictions a little; the day says "tells Ama a story" / "hears a story from Jon". **Passed down the generations**: a child who heard it tells their own children, less true again. Stories and retellings throw their own dice (the "stories" stream: they do not change what everyone does next).
- [x] **Cultural memory** (`scripts/history/cultural_memory.gd`, `session.culture`, `ctx.culture`), once a game day: when **three or more of a settlement's people (and 30% of them)** hold a memory of the same thing made of it the same way, the settlement remembers it together — as strong as the share who hold it ("The people here now tell each other of rain falling out of a clear sky — a spirit's doing"; a flood: "The people here remember the flood together"); unheld, it fades (3% a day) and is forgotten. **It colours how its people take the like of it** (interpretation + 0.8 × its strength).
- [x] **Myths v0** (bible §15.4): **each retelling may change the story** (`scripts/perception/lore.gd`; 25% of tellings for the most creative teller, half that for the least): it **grows** ("…, greater than anyone had seen"; told with 20% more force each time), moves back to **the first days** ("…, in the first days"), or **becomes someone's doing** (what was nobody's becomes a spirit's). What a story has become travels with it (stimulus → memory → the next telling). **When a cultural memory takes the like of something to be someone's doing** (a spirit's, a god's, an ancestor's, an unknown mind's), holds at least 30% of the settlement, **and its people have lived through it on three days or more**, **a myth forms**: an epithet ("the Rainbringer", "the Unseen Hand", "the Thrower of Stones" … — a name of its own comes with the language, M17), what it is behind, how it is felt (benevolent, fearsome or capricious, from what its believers felt), its believers (kept up to date), where it showed itself, when it formed: "A myth has taken shape: the Rainbringer, the kindly one behind rain falling out of a clear sky" (caused by the cultural memory it grew out of).
- [x] **Save format 23** (was 22): the cultural memories, the days lived through, the myths; what a story has become is saved with the memory. Migration 22 → 23, real fixture `tests/fixtures/saves/v22_world.sav` (one dead, in a grave), test.
- **Deviations:**
  - **A myth's sacred places, rituals, splitting, merging and debunking** are later (M19's culture and religion); its name is an epithet until the language (M17). Literacy (which would make retelling truer) does not exist yet.
  - The life memories are templates with the other person's name; their own words for each kind of memory come with M17/M19.
  - **The untouched world tells few stories** (30 years without the player: 5 bedtime stories held, cultural memories only of its two floods — taken by most as a god's doing — and no myth, which needs three days lived through). Stories come mostly from what the player does — as the bible means them to.
- **Caught while building:**
  - **Memories did not last** (see above).
  - **Stories threw the people's dice** (the "ai" stream): a bedtime story changed what everyone did next (a routine test saw two at breakfast, not three). They have their own stream now.
  - The words of a story that had changed in the telling read "in the first days, was told at bedtime by …" (the telling put in the first days): what the story became is now part of what was told ("… of a touch from an unseen hand, in the first days — …").
  - An earlier edit (M10.2) had left a line break inside a debug string (`main.gd`); fixed while adding the culture's line.
- **Numbers:** 30 game years of the untouched default world (`soak_m7.gd -- --years=30`, final code): 8 → 13 people (8 … 18), 10 born, 7 died (7 graves); **83 memories held at the end** (none before lasting memories); **the settlement remembers its two floods together** — as a god's doing (12 of 13 people, strength 0.92), a spirit's (10), nothing strange (9), an unseen mind's (4); **5 bedtime stories** held; no myth (two floods, three days needed); 0.96 ms a game minute; SOAK PASSED. Before lasting memories the same soak ended with 0 memories, 0 cultural memories and 0 stories.
- **Verified:** `test_stories_and_myths` (10): `test_life_memories`, **`test_family_memory_transmission`** (a story at bedtime: its words, when it happened, less true, not twice, not from afar; told on to a grandchild, less true again; thirty listeners make different things of it), `test_a_story_at_bedtime_in_the_running_world` (the real evening), `test_grandparents_tell_their_grandchildren`, `test_stories_change_in_the_telling` (the three changes, their words and force; carried in a telling), **`test_cultural_pool_formation`** (two hold it: not yet; three: the settlement's; its words; it colours interpretation by exactly its strength × 0.8; it fades when unheld; a flood together), **`test_myth_formation`** (a spiritual band; rain out of a clear sky in the running world, through perception: a myth after 3 rains — "the Rainbringer", a spirit, benevolent, 8 believers; once), `test_what_was_lived_strongly_lasts`, `test_the_words_of_a_story`, **`test_history_persistence`** (culture, myths and what stories became saved; a version-22 world; broken records). **Full suite: 1163 passed, 0 failed (`test_a_whole_day_of_the_band` 0.962 ms a game minute).** Desktop screenshots, Mobile and Compatibility (`C:/tmp/m111/`): the toasts of a cultural memory and a myth; a child's card with a bedtime story that grew in the telling.

### M11.2 Historical record & significance — ✅ DONE (2026-10-03) — phone check outstanding
(Before the VS gate, at the owner's word: VS.1–VS.6 are still open.)
- [x] **Significance** (`scripts/history/significance.gd`, `session.significance`; `SignificanceConfig`, `data/configuration/significance_config.tres`, `Config.significance`): nobody is important to begin with (bible §21.5). **Each event adds to the significance of those who took part in it** — the first of its participants its whole significance (the event weights are data: `data/events/*.tres`), the others 40% — and **a first adds 0.5** to its principal. **History is made of what stands out**: an event that matters less than 0.35 adds nothing (a day's hunt, a chat) unless it is a first; **the same kind of deed counts less each time** (the n-th time 1 / (1 + 0.5 (n − 1))); **one's own life with others is one's own** (friends, quarrels, fights, partners, making up, being taken in add nothing), nor does what merely happens to someone (born, coming of age, hurt, ill, their death). **Being touched by the Presence counts 0.15 each time** (bible §16.3: "touched by the Presence 20 times may become a historical figure"; taken-together touches count each). Significance is a sum of points on the person (saved since M3), kept in the archive when they die (with a long life and children added: M10.3), and an obituary matters as much as they did (up to the threshold).
- [x] **The firsts ledger**: the event log's first of each kind (kept since M6) as `Significance.firsts()` — those marked first, the earliest first. **More firsts**: the first death, storm, flood, drought, shortage, fire gone out, couple, friendship, fight, newcomer, and **the player's first of each kind of act** (first touch, first rain …), with the first child born, first hunt of each animal, first of each trade, first cultural memory and first myth.
- [x] **Important people v0**: from **2.5 points** someone is one of the Important People — "{name} is someone people speak of now" (caused by what tipped it). **☰ → PEOPLE → Important People** and **☰ → HISTORY → Important People** (living, and the dead "… (died in year 12)"; the most significant first; under each, what they are most remembered for — their most significant deed, as the chronicle tells it); a tap goes to them (or to their grave). **☰ → HISTORY → Firsts** lists the firsts ("YEAR 1 · Treya has died, old and full of years"). The AI inspector shows someone's points.
- **Deviations:**

### M11 — Completion summary
All four sub-phases are done (M11.1–M11.4) — **before the VS gate, at the owner's word (VS.1–VS.6 still open)**: memory beyond the moment (life's moments, stories at bedtime, cultural memory, myths), significance and the Important People, the timeline, and the player's own history with what came of it. **1175 passed, 0 failed (`test_a_whole_day_of_the_band` 0.988 ms)** (1153 at the start of M11).
- **Automated tests named in the plan:** `test_family_memory_transmission`, `test_cultural_pool_formation`, `test_myth_formation` (repeated rain from a clear sky in a spiritual band forms "the Rainbringer" after 3 rains, through perception), `test_history_persistence` (`test_stories_and_myths.gd`); `test_significance_firsts` (`test_significance.gd`); `test_timeline_locate_fallbacks` (`test_timeline.gd`).
- **Manual** ("make rain during 3 droughts → a myth forms; check a grandchild's memory of the grandparent's touch"): **not done by hand.** Both are automated in parts: a myth from three rains in the running world (`test_myth_formation`); a story told on to a grandchild, less true again, and the act it goes back to kept (`test_family_memory_transmission`, `test_who_remembers_what_i_did`).
- **Soak:** 30 game years of the untouched world after each sub-phase: memories last (83 held at the end), the floods are remembered together (as a god's doing by most), bedtime stories are told, **one** Important Person in thirty years; every invariant held.
- **Phone (Note20):** the builds of M10.4, M11.1, M11.2 and M11.3 were installed at the owner's word (each with the save backed up first); **none of M11 has been looked at on the phone by me.** The M11.4 build is not on it.
- **Save format:** 22 → 23 (cultural memory and myths, with a real fixture); what a story became and which act a memory goes back to are optional fields of the memory.
- **Exit criterion** — *"The world begins generating stories naturally — a tester can read the timeline and retell a story that wasn't scripted."* — **the material is there; whether a tester can retell a story from it is for a tester to say (VS.6).** The timeline holds the world's year-by-year history in words, with causes ("…, at odds for a while, have come to blows"; "the first child born here"; "has died of the wounds of a fight"); people tell their children what they lived through, the stories change in the telling, a settlement comes to remember things together and turns the Presence's repeated doings into myths; the player's own history shows who remembers each act, and what came of it. **Without the player the world tells few stories** (storms and floods taken as nature's, bar the floods taken as a god's doing) — by design: the stories are mostly of what the player does.
- **Still to judge by hand:** how readable the timeline is when long (Major as the default?); how often stories change in the telling; the flood's cultural memory split four ways (one memory with rival explanations?); the Important People's threshold (2.5 points); the menu on the phone.
- **Not in M11 on purpose:** the story engine's chains and named eras (M19); myths' rituals and sacred places (M19); writing (M18); discoveries and wars in the timeline's filters (later milestones).

### M11 — Tests & checks
**Automated:** `test_family_memory_transmission`, `test_cultural_pool_formation`, `test_myth_formation` (repeated rain-in-drought in a spiritual population forms a myth within N events), `test_significance_firsts`, `test_timeline_locate_fallbacks`, `test_history_persistence`.
**Manual:** make rain during 3 droughts → a myth forms; check a grandchild's memory of the grandparent's touch (REMEMBERED hook).
**Exit criteria:** *The world begins generating stories naturally* — a tester can read the timeline and retell a story that wasn't scripted.

## M12 — BASIC CIVILIZATION

**Goal:** Move beyond a village: recognizable communities with different characteristics. (P:M12, S§26, B§17)
**Depends on:** M11. **Split into 5 sub-phases.**

### M12.1 Settlement planner & construction — ✅ DONE (2026-10-03) — phone check outstanding
(Before the VS gate, at the owner's word: VS.1–VS.6 are still open.)
- [x] **The settlement decides what to build** (`scripts/civilization/settlement_planner.gd`, once a game day after 08:00): **homes** when the roofs are full (fewer than one place left), when **two households share a roof with no room left for a child** (births need room in the mother's home), or when someone has no roof (theirs fell) — but not while a home stands empty (each day, a crowded household moves into an empty home first), **a storehouse** when the stores overflow (room for fewer than 8 more) or food has gone bad (12 units in 3 days) and there are fewer than 1 + people/12, **a well** when the water to drink is more than 14 tiles from the fire. One building at a time — **but a home does not wait for anything else**. **Where:** open, dry grass or dirt 3–11 tiles from the fire, **above the highest water the settlement remembers**, a tile clear all round (no tree or bush grows into its walls; no other building, fire, field), 3 tiles from any grave, not where the stores are kept, reachable; the most even and nearest wins.
- [x] **Building** (`scripts/civilization/construction_system.gd`): a project is a site (a new prop: **stakes and a line, then from 40% a frame of posts and half-raised walls**), the materials it needs, what has been brought, the work done (strokes) and who did it. **Builders bring the materials themselves** — from the stores (not the seed grain kept back), and **stone that is not in the stores from the stones lying about** (a loose rock or pebble near the site that can be walked to: it is gone from the world, in their arms, and at the site) — and **build only as far as what has been brought allows**. The builder (an occupation now, no longer a placeholder: taken up when there is something to build — and, when a home is to be built or mended or someone is without a roof, by whoever is fittest even if nobody can be spared) knocks at it (the knock of a touched building, seen and heard); builders grow more skilled (+0.05 a building). Done: the building stands; **a home goes to a household** — one without a roof first, else one of a home that two households share (or that is over-full).
- [x] **Buildings** (`data/buildings/*.tres`, `BuildingDef`: prop kind, footprint, materials, labour, capacity, tags, tech, era variants): **hut** (12 wood, 180 strokes, 6 people, *home*), **storehouse** (16 wood + 6 stone, 240, holds 60 more, *storage*: food goes off half as fast with one standing), **well** (12 stone + 2 wood, 200, *water*: people drink at it), **workshop** (defined; waits for toolmaking: M12.4). New props SITE, STOREHOUSE, WELL, WORKSHOP (a raised log store under a hipped thatch, a ring of stones under a little roof, an open shed with a bench); all solid to walkers.
- [x] **Wear, repair and ruin:** buildings have a **condition** (saved; the inspect card says "Sound", "Weathered (72%)", "Damaged", "Falling apart"; a site says "A hut, 45% built" and what it is waiting for). **A flood takes 300 from a building standing in water, a storm 40 from each**; below 700 a repair is begun (a quarter of the materials and half the work, for the wear there is); at nothing the building falls — a **ruin** — and its household moves where there is room. **A home nobody lives in for two game years begins to fall apart, and is a ruin two years later.**
- [x] **History:** "Work has begun on the first hut", "The first hut stands, built by Glaiwo", "The flood has damaged the hut", "Ufok has mended the hut", "An empty hut has fallen into ruin" (firsts by building; damage only when it needs mending, not every storm's wear). Day log: "builds", "breaks stone".
- **Deviations:** **no `construction_view.tscn`**: the stages are the site prop's look (stakes → frame → the building), drawn by the prop mesher like every other prop. **"Farm plots"** are M7.4's fields (the farmer already makes them), not the planner's. **"Sacredness"** of a site is distance from graves for now (sacred places are M19). **No earthquakes** in the game yet: floods and storms do the damage. A storehouse **adds room to the stores and slows spoiling; the piles stay by the fire** (people do not yet carry food into it). The workshop waits for toolmaking (M12.4, M18). Footprints are one tile.
- **Caught while building:** **(1) Stone never came** (30-year soaks, both seeds: a storehouse stuck at 73% for decades, blocking every other building): these worlds have no rock props, only loose rocks, so "gather stone at a rock" found nothing — and the builder chose that job and stood idle. Builders now bring stones lying about; the nearest stone was across the river — now it is the nearest *reachable* one. **(2) New huts stood empty and fell** (8 built, 7 ruined in 30 years): only a new couple ever moved into a home. Now a household moves in when it is finished, no home is planned while one stands empty, and the decay clock was far too quick (a game year is 24 days: "12 days empty" was half a year). **(3) A world lost every roof** (100-year soak of seed 4242, traced year by year): the band stalled at 15 for twenty years — one hut held 7, another 3, and births need room in the mother's own home — then aged; when the last builder grew old nobody mended the huts, and storms wore them all to ruins (the band had dwindled the same way before M12: 5 people after 100 years at `80e2a02`). Now crowded shared roofs call for a home and the household moves; empty homes are lived in again; a home to build or mend makes whoever is fittest a builder; the roofless move in first. The same seed: 20 people in 5 huts at year 42. **(4) Every storm wrote a line per building** (147 in 30 years). (5) Builders would have taken the seed grain from the stores (`available`, not `amount`). (6) The storehouse's roof was flat, and a frame was drawn round a bush (screenshots): a hipped roof; the site keeps a clear tile all round. (7) A backslash continuation joined by an edit script, caught by grep.
- **Verified:** `test_construction` (12): `test_the_buildings_are_defined`, **`test_construction_consumes_materials`** (fetched from the stores, delivered, built only as far as brought, stakes → frame → hut, the builders credited and more skilled, the first hut in words), **`test_planner_proposes_storage_when_overflow`** (and from food going bad; a home does not wait; the storehouse's room), `test_a_hut_when_the_homes_are_full_and_a_well_when_water_is_far`, `test_the_planner_weighs_once_a_day_by_daylight`, **`test_building_damage_repair`** (wear, a flood's damage in words, the repair, worn through → ruin; a lived-in home falls → room elsewhere, or a roof again when the next hut stands), `test_a_crowded_household_moves_into_a_new_home` (an empty home is lived in again; two households under a full roof call for a home; one moves in), `test_an_empty_home_falls_into_ruin`, `test_building_is_saved`, `test_the_band_builds_what_was_begun` (the running world), `test_stone_lying_about_is_brought_to_the_site`, `test_version_23_save_loads` (a real v23 fixture from `80e2a02`). Changed: `test_occupations` (the builder is real), `test_ui_panels` (a hut's card shows its condition, not a size), `test_interaction_manager` (the new kinds' touch). **Full suite: 1187 passed, 0 failed (`test_a_whole_day_of_the_band` 0.995 ms).** Numbers (final code): **30 game years, seed 12345: 23 people (13 at M11.4) in 7 huts and a storehouse, 5 buildings built, none fallen; seed 777: 19 people in 6 huts and a storehouse; 100 game years, seed 4242: 7 people at the end, 22 at most, 5 huts and a storehouse standing (at `80e2a02` the same seed: 5 people, 16 at most); all SOAK PASSED.** **Food is tighter in the bigger bands:** the stores below 0.4 days 122 times in 30 years (16 at M11.4, 13 people); seed 4242 had one shortage of about ten game days (2 at `80e2a02`), worst health 0.25 (0.43). Desktop screenshots, Mobile and Compatibility (`C:/tmp/m121/`): the village with a storehouse, well, workshop and new hut; stakes; a frame with its builder; the storehouse's card.
- **Save format:** 23 → 24 (projects, what the planner remembers; a building's condition is saved only when it is not sound). Older worlds build from the day they are opened.
- **Phone (Note20):** not installed (the owner asked for M12.2 without an install).

### M12.2 Roads & traffic — ✅ DONE (2026-10-03) — phone check outstanding
(Before the VS gate, at the owner's word: VS.1–VS.6 are still open.)
- [x] **Footfall** (`scripts/civilization/traffic.gd`): every step onto a tile is counted (the MovementSystem tells it); once a game day the day's steps are added to what the tile remembers, and the memory fades (0.9 of it is kept a day: what one day's walking leaves is gone in about a year).
- [x] **Paths:** grass or bare earth walked enough (remembered footfall 30 — about three steps a day, kept up) **is worn to a path** (the ROAD terrain: bare earth, a new colour; "Path" on the card) — not a field, not under a tree, bush or building, not in water. The pathfinder already walks a path faster (0.7 of open ground), so **a path draws feet to it** and the network settles on the ways people take. **A path walked less than 8 grows over** to what it was. The ground's card says how much it is walked ("Footfall: Well trodden — a path"). History notes the first ("Feet have worn the first path through the grass").
- [x] **Bridges:** a ford — water one wades through — waded enough (40) **calls for a bridge**; the settlement planner begins it (after homes, storage and water), **built where it will stand** (posts, then beams, then a deck with a rail; people wade past it meanwhile), turned across the water (the shorter way to dry land). Built: **dry-shod over the ford** (the ford walks like a path, people stand on the deck), 10 wood, 150 strokes. Floods harm a bridge only when the water is too deep to wade, storms as any building; worn through, **it is washed away** (nothing is left in the ford). History: "The first bridge stands, built by …", "The bridge has been washed away".
- [x] Builders work on **homes first** (built or mended), then the rest.
- **Deviations:** **"road" is the worn path** — laid roads come with technology (M18), as the plan says. A bridge is **one tile**: a wide ford is bridged tile by tile as each is waded enough (no long spans, nothing over deep water). Paths are drawn as whole tiles (the terrain is per tile).
- **Caught while building:** **(1) Bridges ran along the river** (screenshot): on a wide river every neighbour of a ford is water — turned now by the shorter water span to dry land. **(2) Worn paths looked like sand** (screenshot): an earthier colour. **(3) A path said "Plant cover: Lush"** (screenshot): not shown on a path. **(4) A bridge stands in water, so every flood damaged it** (8 bridges and ~75 repairs in 30 years, the builder kept from homes): only water over the ford harms it now, and homes come first. (5) The edit script, rerun after a half-applied run, doubled six inserted blocks (caught by the parse check; the lesson is in my notes).
- **Verified:** `test_traffic` (8): **`test_traffic_to_road`** (a few steps a day: no path; walked daily: worn to a path, quicker to walk, the first in history, the ground's card), `test_a_path_nobody_walks_grows_over`, `test_only_open_ground_is_worn` (not a field, not under a bush), `test_a_path_draws_feet` (the way found follows the path), **`test_a_bridge_over_a_busy_ford`** (waded → wanted → begun in the ford, people still pass; posts → beams → deck; dry-shod; someone on it stands on the deck; washed away by a flood, nothing left), `test_paths_are_saved`, `test_the_band_wears_paths` (eight days of the running world wear paths about the settlement), `test_version_24_save_loads` (a real v24 fixture from `44f71fe`). Changed: `test_construction` (the bridge among the buildings), `test_interaction_manager`. **Full suite: 1191 passed, 4 failed** with six soaks running beside it — the bridge's posts reached 2 cm below the ground (`test_prop_mesher`, fixed), and three that time the machine (`test_a_whole_day_of_the_band` 1.138 ms, `test_a_month_of_housekeeping` over 30 s, `test_people_take_turns_within_a_tick`); **run again alone, all pass** (`test_a_whole_day_of_the_band` 0.942 ms). Numbers (final code), 30 game years: **seed 12345: 13 people, 6 huts, 165 path tiles, 4 bridges; 777: 13, 4 huts, 142 paths, 8 bridges; 2024: 26, 7 huts, 242 paths, 5 bridges; 99: 14, 4 huts, 112 paths, 4 bridges; 31337: 13, 4 huts, 116 paths, 6 bridges** — all SOAK PASSED. The same five seeds at M12.1 (`44f71fe`): 23, 19, 18, 15, 11 people (mean 17.2; now 15.8 — one 30-year run per seed varies much; the flood fix brought seed 777 from 8 back to 13). **100 game years, seed 4242: 13 people, 5 generations** (7 at M12.1, 5 before M12), 152 paths (378 worn, 226 grown over in the century), 14 bridges; SOAK PASSED — **but its hunger is the worst yet: a shortage of about 17 game days, worst health 0.07** (M12.1: 10 days, 0.25). ### M12.3 Migration & multiple settlements — ✅ DONE (2026-10-03) — phone check outstanding
(Before the VS gate, at the owner's word: VS.1–VS.6 are still open.)
- [x] **More than one settlement** (`scripts/civilization/settlements.gd`): the world keeps every settlement — the first, and those founded since — each with **its own fire, homes, stores, job board, places and planner**. People belong to one (`PersonData.settlement_id`); **whatever someone does, they do as one of their own settlement** (`AiContext.enter` switches the context to it at every turn: their stores, their places, their fields, their plans). Built for it: construction projects belong to a settlement (homes go to its homes; each planner sees to its own — storehouses and wells near its fire, bridges nearer its fire than another's); builders work on their own settlement's projects; farmers work their own settlement's fields (`Farming.ours`; plots and farmers counted per settlement); the dead are buried near their own fire; households move within their settlement (taken in elsewhere, one belongs there now); newcomers come to where the lonely one lives; animals keep away from every fire; what has been explored is known to all (the land those who set out knew); other settlements' fires give light at night.
- [x] **Migration** (`scripts/civilization/migration.gd`): once a game day each settlement of 10 or more weighs **what drives people away** — **crowding** (two places or fewer left under the roofs), **hunger** (short of food four days on end), **strife** (rivals and enemies among them), **disaster** (floods lived through lately), and **the adventurous** (its most adventurous households) — and, past a threshold, by the dice, **a group sets out**: one household or two with a man and a woman grown among them, the most adventurous, leaving at least six behind; then nobody from there for four game years. **Where to: only ground someone has explored** (Places' visited cells — exploration matters): even, dry, open ground above the water the settlement remembers, water near (not on the bank), trees near, 14 tiles or more from every settlement, 40 at most from home; the best of it. They take food (three days' each, at most 40% of the stores) and wood, and **walk there, nothing else on their minds**; on arrival **they found a settlement**: a fire, a first hut from the wood they carried, the food they brought — it is theirs, a camp like the first, and its own planner builds on. At most four settlements.
- [x] **Founders and names:** the leader — the most adventurous — founds it: "Rishun is the first to set out north with one other to find new land", then "**Rishun has founded Rishun's camp**" (the founding is caused by the setting out, which is caused by what drove them: "Driven by hunger, …", "After the floods, …", "Leaving strife behind, …"). ### M12.4 Trade & specialization — ✅ DONE (2026-10-03) — phone check outstanding
(Before the VS gate, at the owner's word: VS.1–VS.6 are still open.)
- [x] **Specialization** emerges: each settlement remembers what it brings in (every load put in its stores; fading 5% a day); **what sets it apart is what it is known for** — among other settlements, what it brings in more of than they do (at least 15% of what it brings in, and 1.15 times its share of what all bring in); alone, the greater part of what it brings in (35%) ("Known for: wood" on its fire's card). **Gathering makes one better at it** (skill grows with every unit), and **the skilled work faster** (up to half again). **The young lean toward their settlement's trade** when they choose one (its weight ×2.5) — so a settlement among trees becomes a settlement of woodcutters, one with fields of farmers.
- [x] **Trade** (`scripts/civilization/trade_system.gd`): once a game day each settlement weighs what it has beyond what it keeps (food for five days, ten of anything else) against what another, within reach on foot, lacks — **its offer: its surplus there, and back what the other can spare that it lacks**. A **trader** (a new occupation, taken up once there is trade to be done; a pack basket: twice the load) carries it, a load at a time, **on foot along the paths** (M12.2's paths draw them), and brings the return load home. **Statistics:** loads by route and by resource ("Trade: sends wood · gets grain" on the card; `TradeSystem.routes`, `totals`, `trips`). History: the first load between two settlements ("Gosce has carried wood from the first camp to Basge's camp: the first trade").
- [x] **Workshops and tools:** someone skilled at their work (0.6) may **work out how to make good tools** (2% a day: "Ama has worked out how to make good tools"; a settlement founded from there knows it too); then, once there are six or more, **the planner wants a workshop** (the M12.1 building, no longer waiting); a **toolmaker** (a new occupation) makes tools there **of wood and stone from the stores** (an hour each) while fewer than one per worker are in store — and, with no stone in store, fetches a stone lying about for it. **Tools make work go faster** (with one for every worker, half again — on top of skill) and wear out in use (one in two hundred units brought in). **Tools are the first "tools" statistic** (`StatsRecorder`: tools in store, every settlement; `Settlement.tools_made`).
- **Deviations:** **"abstracted when not visible"** is not needed yet — the box is small and every settlement lives in full; the abstraction comes with the larger world (M13+). "Mining" and "fishing" specializations come with those trades (there are no miners or fishers yet: stone is picked up by builders, fish not yet caught); a settlement can be known for wood, berries, grain or meat (or stone, without a trade to lean toward). Tools are one kind of thing (better ones with technology, M18) and lie in the stores like wood (their pile looks like wood). Crafting draws the wood and stone from the stores when the tool is done (the toolmaker does not carry them over).
- **Caught while building:** (1) **a trader carried four logs and the day's offer was spent** (screenshot run): the offer now stays, less each load, for the next run. **(2) Every settlement was known for berries** (30-year soaks: foraging is the greater part everywhere): now what sets it apart from the others. **(3) Tools never stayed in store** (soaks: 1–3 made, none left) **— and a storehouse stuck at 73% again in two worlds**: **the stones lying about run out** (36–40 within reach, in a box of 500–1,000 tiles of rocky ground), so the toolmaker had none and builders none either. **Stone is now broken from rocky ground** once none lies about (40 minutes for a load: the start of quarrying); the toolmaker fetches it for the stores; tools wear a quarter as fast. (4) A name declared twice in the soak's new report line kept every soak from starting (twelve, unnoticed for 40 minutes: now checked within a minute). (5) Offers were handed out as the stored record, so a caller could change them (caught by a test): copies now. (6) "The First Camp" (the card capitalised every word). (7) Tools had no pile of their own (a test: every resource has one): handles with stone heads.

### M12.5 Governance v0 — ✅ DONE (2026-10-03) — phone check outstanding
(Before the VS gate, at the owner's word: VS.1–VS.6 are still open.)
- [x] **A leader emerges** in every settlement of three or more (`scripts/civilization/governance.gd`, weighed once a game day): **the one its people look to most** — the respect of their neighbours (earned by teaching, M10) and their liking, their years (best about forty), **having founded it**, what they have done (significance points, M11.2), and their nature (the ambitious, the social). A leader is **replaced only by someone clearly more looked to** (a margin), and otherwise leads **until they die or leave** (one who sets out to found a settlement leaves the old one's lead behind).
- [x] **What a leader brings:** **their people's interpretations lean toward the leader's beliefs** (the cultural prior: a settlement's character follows its leader — what they make of the Presence's doings); **their nature tilts the settlement's plans**: an ambitious leader presses on with building (the building job half again as pressing) and plans homes with a place more to spare; a cautious one keeps more food back from trade, a generous one less.
- [x] **Succession is history:** "Teireso has come to lead the first camp", "After the death of Oukimsu, Eibrust leads the first camp" (caused by the death), "With Kodo gone, Rako leads Pakest's camp", "Ama has taken over the lead of the first camp from Hoto". Leading is significant (0.6; the first 0.75): leaders come to be people people speak of. The fire's card says who leads ("Led by").
- [x] **A settlement nobody is left at is abandoned** (found in the 150-year soak: a camp emptied and lingered with nobody, and a hut it would never build): its fire goes cold, what it was building is left undone (its sites taken away), its huts are nobody's and fall into ruin in time, and history says so ("Nobody is left at Vuyu's camp: it has been abandoned"). The first settlement is never abandoned, even empty. (Whether the last few of a dwindling settlement should move to another is still the owner's to decide.)

Taken apart: a fifth of the time was the settlements' housekeeping, and nearly all of that was **one search — where the next field plot goes — repeated at every job board's refresh in sowing time** (3.6 ms each, 48 a day). ### M12 — Completion summary
All five sub-phases are done (M12.1–M12.5) — **before the VS gate, at the owner's word (VS.1–VS.6 still open)**: the settlement plans and builds (homes, storehouses, wells, workshops, bridges), wears paths, sends people out to found new settlements, trades between them, specializes, works out toolmaking, and comes to be led. **1218 tests pass** (1187 at the start of M12); `test_a_whole_day_of_the_band` at 0.877 ms of its 1.0 (it had reached 1.004 before M12.5's speed-up).
- **Automated tests named in the plan:** `test_planner_proposes_storage_when_overflow`, `test_construction_consumes_materials`, `test_building_damage_repair` (`test_construction.gd`); `test_traffic_to_road` (`test_traffic.gd`); `test_migration_founds_settlement` (`test_migration.gd`); `test_trade_moves_surplus` (`test_trade.gd`); `test_leader_emergence` (`test_governance.gd`) — all there, all pass.
- **Soak — 150 game years ("≥ 2 settlements typically form on River Valley seeds; no deadlocks; invariants"):** three seeds, **every invariant held, no deadlock** (since M12.1: nobody stuck, every building finished or abandoned, everyone of a settlement that is and under its roofs). **Second settlements: in two of three 150-year runs (4242, 99), and in three of five 30-year runs (777, 99, 31337) — not in 12345 or 2024**; both 150-year camps were founded, dwindled and (with the final code) were abandoned. So "≥ 2 settlements typically form" — **yes, they form; they do not yet last a century**: the founding group is one or two households, and the first settlement's pull (trade, a bigger pool of partners) is stronger.
- **Manual** ("watch a village grow; see a road form; watch a migration group found a new hamlet"): **not done by hand** — the phone has the M12.5 build (installed, not opened by me); each is shown in desktop screenshots (a village with its new buildings, worn paths and a bridge, a new camp founded at the end of a walk) and in the running-world tests.
- **Exit criterion** — *"The world contains recognizable communities with different characteristics."* — **met in the soaks, to be judged by eye:** settlements differ in what they are known for (the first for berries, the camps founded from it for wood), in who leads them and so in what their people make of things (each leans toward its leader's beliefs; each has its own cultural memory), in size and tier, in what they know, and in what they trade (wood to the new camps). How recognizable that is on the phone is the owner's to judge.
- **Still to judge by hand:** **whether founded camps should last** (bigger groups, or their own newcomers); hunger over a century (the longest shortages grew as bands grew: 17–20 game days in seed 4242); how often people migrate (three seeds in five within 30 years); trade that is nearly all wood; storms' wear (40–90 repairs in 30 years); whether a dwindling settlement's last few should move to another (asked, open); the timing budget (recovered in M12.5: 0.87 of 1.0 ms).
- **Not in M12 on purpose:** laid roads and long bridges (technology, M18); trade abstracted out of sight (the larger world, M13+); laws, councils, wars (M19+); names for settlements (M17).

### M12 — Follow-up at the owner's word (2026-10-03): lasting camps, the last few, the time budget
The owner decided: **founded camps should last; the last few of a settlement that cannot grow should move; hunger will be solved later; and a full timing optimisation.**
- [x] **Founded camps last:** (1) **a founding group is two households at least, and five people** (it was one family: a camp of two could never pair its children); (2) **the lonely of a camp are lonely** — whether there is anyone to be partners with is now asked of their own settlement (it was asked of the whole world, so nobody at a camp ever counted as lonely while the first camp had someone, and no newcomer ever came to a camp); newcomers come to a young camp's lonely **three times as readily**; **a settlement of twenty or more feels the push to send people out** whatever its roofs (the bible's overcrowding); **with nowhere known to go, its explorers scout 16 tiles further** (in seeds 12345 and 4242 the only good land lay ~26 tiles out, beyond the 20 anyone explored — so nobody ever set out there); (3) **followers**: when a crowded settlement sends people away and a young camp (fewer than ten) is within reach, they **join it** rather than found yet another ("Ama has gone to join Rishun's camp with 3 others"); (4) a couple of two settlements now belongs to the one whose roof they share (it kept the other's).
- [x] **The last few move:** a settlement that **cannot grow** — no grown-up under elder age, or fewer than four and no couple young enough for children — sends everyone, with what food it has, to **the nearest settlement that can** ("The last of Vuyu's camp have gone to live at the first camp"); under its roofs (the roomiest, crowded if need be — its planner sees to more). A founded camp left empty is then abandoned (M12.5). (The first settlement can empty this way too; it is kept, as the first, even empty.)
- [x] **The time budget — a full pass** (`test_a_whole_day_of_the_band`, the band's day per game minute): **1.004 → 0.723 ms (−28%), with the same world to the decision** (678 decisions). Profiled system by system and inside a person's turn: (1) **the next field plot** was searched for at every job board's refresh (3.6 ms each; M12.5); (2) **a settlement's housekeeping ran at every half-minute step — and in the running game at every frame**: once a tick now; (3) the **fire's burning** looked everything up every tick: nothing to do until the next log is due; (4) **a person's needs factors** (age, sociability, ambition) were worked out at every turn: once a day; (5) **the body's upkeep** (hunger's hardship, the cold, health) every 5 game minutes in one go, not at every turn; (6) the ground was looked at for everyone at every turn: once a tick; (7) the step under way was looked up twice a turn: once. **Tried and left out:** giving people turns by their step's patience in `BehaviorSystem.step` too (as the running game's SimulationManager does) would reach 0.59 ms — but it changes what people do in the tests' and soaks' world (lunch together, bedtimes, talk), so not without the owner's word; calm animals looking about every 5 minutes instead of 3 — they noticed people too late.
- **Verified:** `test_migration` (13, +4): `test_a_founding_group_is_two_households_at_least`, `test_followers_join_a_young_camp` (no new settlement; under the camp's roof; in words), `test_the_last_few_move_where_they_can_grow` (one alone cannot grow; they go to the first, under its roof; in words; the camp abandoned), `test_a_camps_lonely_get_newcomers`. **Full suite: 1221 passed, 2 failed** beside eight soaks (the two that time the machine; run alone they pass). **Soaks — 30 years, five seeds: all PASSED** (camps founded in 99 and 31337 hold 7 and 8 people at year 30). **150 years, before the size push and scouting:** seed 99 — two camps founded, eight groups joined camps, the last few of one camp moved to the first and that camp was abandoned, **Glotha's camp still alive at year 150**; seeds 12345 and 4242 — nobody ever set out (the cause found afterwards: nowhere explored to go).
- **Revisited (owner, 2026-10-04):** the follow-up was committed (`2b7a392`); its 150-year round was begun and **stopped at the owner's word for bridges first** — "having the bridge ability may also aid [finding a new place to build a settlement] as well as the extended search" — and run again on code with them (below).

### M12 — Follow-up 2 at the owner's word (2026-10-04): bridges across, bank to bank
The owner saw on the phone that **the bridges stopped at the first tile**: four of them, side by side on the river's shallow east edge, crossing nothing (M12.2's bridge was one tile over a ford waded often — on a river with a deep middle, nobody wades the middle, so it never got the next tile; the four were called for by people walking along the edge, not across). The owner: they "should be able to keep building the bridge tile by tile until reaching the other side."
- [x] **A crossing** (`scripts/civilization/crossing.gd`; `SettlementPlanner.crossing`): **land near the fire (24 tiles) that cannot be reached on foot** — at least 12 tiles of it, once the settlement is six strong — calls for a bridge across: **the shortest way straight over from land that can be reached to land that cannot, at most 8 tiles of water, deep water too**, nearest the fire. **Built tile by tile from the near bank** (one at a time; the builder stands on the bank or on the deck laid before); **a crossing begun is carried on to the far bank** (even the last shallow tile, which could be waded) — and an old one-tile bridge is a start (on the owner's world the crossing starts at the stub at (0, 0) and runs west over four tiles). **One way over a stretch of water**: a stub within 6 tiles of a finished crossing is not carried on (the other three stay as little piers). Once across, the far side can be walked to and nothing more is wanted. (The busy-ford trigger of M12.2 is gone.)
- [x] **The deck lies level with the higher bank**, posts reaching down to the bed (`PropMesher`: a post stretched to each corner); the pathfinder takes a deck as dry ground at its banks' level over whatever water is below; people on it stand on the deck; **only water up to the deck harms it** (a river under it is no flood).
- **The owner's world** (from the phone's save, year 22): the crossing is planned from the stub and the second tile goes up — but the world is **short of wood** (about 9 a day from three woodcutters; huts, repairs after storms, a storehouse and the fire all want it, and a bridge comes last) and **hungry** (a day of food: "hunger will be solved later"); a flood on day 15 of my run took the stubs and the half-built tile, and the planner began again from the bank. It will get there as the wood allows.
- **Deviations:** the owner did not choose among my questions (what calls for a crossing; what becomes of the four stubs): I took my recommendations — the need for the far side; one stub carried on, the rest left. Long bridges were "technology, M18" in the plan; the owner brought them forward. No stone bridges, no arches: wood, 10 a tile.
- **Verified:** `test_crossing` (5): **`test_a_crossing_bank_to_bank`** (planned from land they can reach to land they cannot, over deep water; tile by tile from the near bank, one at a time, each worked from beside; then the far bank is reached over it and nothing more is wanted; the deck at the higher bank, someone on it above the water; drawn with its posts), `test_a_one_tile_bridge_is_carried_on`, `test_a_crossing_needs_land_cut_off_and_a_short_way`, `test_only_water_up_to_the_deck_harms_it` (and washed away), `test_four_stubs_in_a_row_make_one_crossing` (the owner's case). Moved: M12.2's busy-ford test (its built/beams/washed-away checks are in `test_crossing`). Changed: `test_construction` and `test_resources` (no crossing wanted where they test other building and work). **Full suite (alone): 1251 passed, 1 failed** — `test_resources` (a woodcutter found work: the crossing’s wood; fixed, 15/15 again; `test_a_whole_day_of_the_band` 0.719 ms). **30-year soaks, all PASSED, a crossing in every world:** seed 4242 — 23 people, 5 bridge tiles; **12345 — 32 people, two camps founded across the water** (Brifoush's and Madi's, 8 each; in the 150-year soak before this, nobody from 12345 ever set out); 99 — 19 people, one camp, four joined it. Desktop screenshots (`C:/tmp/m12x/`): a crossing with someone on it, and its posts in the water.
- **150 years, seeds 4242, 12345, 99 (the round the follow-up waited for):**
- **12345: SOAK PASSED — 62 people (peak 64), 7 generations; three camps founded across the water and all four settlements alive at year 150, each a hamlet now** (the first camp 12, Brifoush's 17, Madi's 18, Laga's 15); 31 people joined camps over the century, none abandoned; two crossings (10 bridge tiles). (Before the size push, the scouting and the crossings, nobody from 12345 ever set out in 150 years.)
  - **99: SOAK PASSED — 22 people (peak 29), 7 generations; Vaiche's camp founded and alive at year 150** (5 people; 22 joined it over the century); one crossing.
  - **4242: SOAK PASSED — but no camp ever formed**: 9 people at year 150 (6 … 23), 7 generations, one crossing (5 tiles); nobody set out even at 23. Its hunger is long: a shortage of about 9 game days at the longest, worst health 0.23.
- [x] **Why 4242 never founded a camp — found and fixed (2026-10-04):** a probe of its first 30 years: ~300 good sites explored by year 7; **in year 11 a shallow flood stood 8 cm in the huts and the settlement remembered it (1.28) — and every site at the camp's own height (1.20) was ruled out, anywhere in the world, for good** (a camp site had to stand above the flood remembered at home); the push to leave was there (0.73–0.87, from 0.6), nowhere to go. **A site is judged by its own water now:** its ground above the highest water near it (`MigrationConfig.flood_clear`, 0.1); the flood remembered at home still keeps that settlement's own huts off its flooded ground. After the flood, ~340 sites remain. (The phone world remembers the same kind of flood: 1.26 over ground at 1.20 — 41% of its dry land counted as flood land for camps.) I first took it for bridges counted as flooded buildings — they are not (only the fire and the huts are watched): that "fix" was undone before it was committed.
  - **Verified:** `test_migration` 14 (+1: `test_a_flood_at_home_does_not_rule_out_land_elsewhere` — the same site chosen with the flood remembered at home; a site under the water near it is none). **30-year soaks, all PASSED: 4242 founds Unsu's camp** (11 people, the first camp 12; before: never in 150 years); 12345 Brifoush's camp; 99 unchanged (Vaiche's camp). **Full suite (alone): 1253 passed, 0 failed** (the band's day 0.716 ms). **The 150-year round with this fix is not run** (the one long round asked for was run above; another waits for the owner's word).
  - **The follow-up is closed** with these (the owner's "finish m12"); 4242’s missing camp: found and fixed (above).

### M12 — Tests & checks
**Automated:** `test_planner_proposes_storage_when_overflow`, `test_construction_consumes_materials`, `test_building_damage_repair`, `test_traffic_to_road`, `test_migration_founds_settlement`, `test_trade_moves_surplus`, `test_leader_emergence`.
**Soak:** 150 game years: ≥ 2 settlements typically form on River Valley seeds; no deadlocks; invariants.
**Manual:** watch a village grow; see a road form; watch a migration group found a new hamlet.
**Exit criteria:** *The world contains recognizable communities with different characteristics.*
