## M13 — CAMERA, MAP AND EXPLORATION SYSTEM

**Goal:** Prepare for a world that grows beyond the initial screen without breaking camera or performance. (P:M13, S§4–7, S§41–42, B§8.6–8.7)
**Depends on:** M12.

### M13.1 Chunk streaming — ✅ DONE (2026-10-03)
(Before the VS gate, at the owner's word: VS.1–VS.6 are still open. The M12 follow-up's ⚠ OPEN item above is still open.)
- [x] `scripts/world/chunk_streamer.gd` (`ChunkStreamer`, owned by WorldView): **only the chunks the camera sees have views** — the ground under the screen's corners (between the box floor and the highest ground: the frustum's footprint), and 6 tiles more; a view is released only 20 tiles beyond that (no flicker at the edge), **to a pool** (up to 32 kept for reuse). The nearest chunks to where the camera looks come first. Looking at the horizon, or anything the corners do not come down on, shows the whole box (the camera is never left with nothing).
- [x] **Mesh building off the main thread:** a chunk coming into sight gets its terrain, water and props built by `WorkerThreadPool` from **copies of the nine chunks it reads** (`WorldData.snapshot_around`, `ChunkData.snapshot`) and of what stands on it (`PropMesher.placements` / `taken_tiles`, read on the main thread; `PropMesher.build_from` touches no registry); the `ArrayMesh`es are made on the main thread, **at most 4 ms of them a frame**. A layer rebuilt in the meantime (the ground changed, water flowed, a tree fell: `ChunkView.versions`) keeps its newer mesh. The first fill is built at once as before (the opening shot frames the whole box), and so are rebuilds of views shown (`refresh_dirty_*`); a chunk out of sight is drawn as it is when it comes back.
- [x] **Measured (desktop, 256×256, river valley):** a chunk's meshes cost ~6 ms on the main thread built at once (terrain 2.9, water 0.9, props 2.0); **streamed, gliding across the box at a working distance: 0.3–0.45 ms a frame on average, 2.4 ms at worst**; close up 16–24 views of 256, 42 at a far zoom. Framed, the whole box is built as before (1.85 s at 256, opening).
- **Deviations:** **chunk data is not evicted** — measured: the soil's passes touch every chunk within a game day (all 256 chunks of a 256-tile world are modified by its first evening), a chunk's data is ~4 KB (1 MB at 256²), and an evicted chunk would be generated again by the next pass (~6 ms each). Dropping a pristine chunk and getting it back identical is in place (`WorldData.unload_chunk`, tested) for when the simulation goes sparse; **simulation activity per chunk tied to the tier manager** waits for that (people already have tiers, M5/M7; the land does not yet). No lower detail for far chunks (M24). The phone's hitch (< 8 ms a chunk) is not yet measured: no phone check was asked for.
- **Verified:** `test_chunk_streaming` (5): **`test_chunk_evict_regenerate_identical`** (every layer, others made in between in another order; a changed chunk stays, with its change), `test_the_framed_box_shows_every_chunk`, **`test_streaming_loads_visible`** (close up: fewer views, every chunk under the screen has one with its ground, the far corner none; across the box the views come from the workers, what was left behind is released; framed again: all 64), `test_a_streamed_chunk_looks_as_one_built_at_once` (terrain, water and props of all 64, vertex for vertex), `test_a_change_while_building_is_not_lost`. **Full suite: 1228 passed, 0 failed** (the band's day: 0.715 ms a game minute, 678 decisions, as before); the streaming and world-view tests again after two guards (a view freed while its build was under way; a finished build not waited for twice).

### M13.2 World growth — the Box Unfolds (D-05) — ✅ DONE (2026-10-04)
(Before the VS gate, at the owner's word: VS.1–VS.4 done, VS.5 skipped for now, VS.6 open. M14 was begun and set aside unwritten at the owner's word.)
- [x] **When** (`scripts/world/box_unfolder.gd`, `BoxUnfolder`, looked at once a game day; bible §8.6): **explorers have reached the walls** — at least a quarter of the band along them (4 tiles deep) has been explored, in Places' cells — **and** the box is **crowded** (6 people to each 1000 tiles: 25 in a box of 64) **or a fire stands within 12 tiles of a wall**; not again for 48 days; never beyond `max_world_tiles` (512). All in `WorldConfig` ("The box unfolds").
- [x] **How** (`WorldSession.unfold`): **a ring of chunks all round** (64 → 96 → 128 …); **the new land is what the generator always makes there** (its shapes are in world coordinates: terrain, water, trees and rocks beyond the old walls are deterministic, the same as a box that began that large); **the world is written down and opened again at its new size** — every part of it (the paths' grid, the water, the soil, the props of the new land, …) is built for the new box the way a saved world is opened, so nothing sized to the old box is left behind. It is done between steps (`unfold_if_due`), never inside the clock's own signal. **History: "The Edge Moved: the walls of the world stand further out than they ever did (64 → 96 tiles across)"** — a major event, for everyone in the world.
- [x] **Seen:** in the game the scene is opened again on the new box (as a world switch, VS.3), the whole box framed, **the walls moving out from where they stood** (3 s; at once with reduced motion), and the player is told.
- [x] **A new world in a larger box:** Settings → Save → New world offers **64 (as usual), 128 or 256 tiles across** (`WorldSession.create_new(seed, size)`).
- **Deviations:** the walls move out over land that is already there (no panels folding open yet: M24's polish). Unfolding opens the world again (a second or two at 96 on desktop) rather than growing each part in place. "Explorers repeatedly reach the wall" is the share of the wall band explored (Places' cells), not counted visits.
- **Verified:** `test_box_unfold` (6): **`test_unfold_extends_bounds_and_pathing`** (a ring all round; everyone and the time kept; the new land the generator's, terrain and props; the paths' grid reaches it and the way leads into it — the west of seed 12345 lies beyond the river, as before; the major event in words; saved and opened at its size), `test_when_the_box_unfolds` (not before the walls are reached; at the walls and crowded; not again so soon; never beyond the largest box), `test_it_unfolds_by_itself_between_steps`, `test_a_new_world_in_a_larger_box`, `test_the_walls_are_seen_to_move` (from where they stood, moving out, where they stand now), `test_in_the_game` (opened again at its size, the walls moving, the whole box in view, the player told). Changed: `test_save_ux` (New world asks how large a box). The soak checks for unfolding each step and says the box's size. **Full suite (alone): 1259 passed, 0 failed** (the band's day 0.719 ms). **30-year soaks, all PASSED (4242, 12345, 99): none of the boxes unfolded in 30 years** — 19–23 people in a box of 64 (25 is crowded) and the walls not yet explored enough; the unfolding itself is shown by the tests and in the running game. A 150-year round to see it come by itself is not run (it waits for the owner's word). Desktop screenshots (`C:/tmp/m132/`): before, the walls moving out over the new land with the toast, after.

### M13.3 Minimap & map — ✅ DONE (2026-10-04)
- [x] **The world from above** (`scripts/ui/map_image.gd`, `MapImage`, built on the debug WorldPreview): **a pixel a tile** — the ground's colour by its kind and height, the water's by its depth — **drawn chunk by chunk**: a chunk whose ground changed (`ground_changed`), whose water moved (`WaterSim.tiles_changed`) or that was newly explored is drawn again, at most 12 a frame (the first drawing spread out so). **What nobody of the world knows is dimmed** (greyed, never black — bible §8.7): known is **where someone has been and what they saw from there** (Places' squares, two all round).
- [x] **The minimap** (`scripts/ui/widgets/minimap.gd`; bottom right, above the journal): the whole box, **the fires of the settlements, the ruins of those before, the one selected (a ring), the latest major events (5) where they happened**, and **what the camera shows, outlined**. **A tap takes the camera there; a drag scrubs it across** (the camera comes closer than the whole box if it has to — framed, it cannot move about). **It folds away to a small button**, and is remembered so (`ui/minimap_open`). Drawn four times a second (what is marked looked at once a second), crisp.
- [x] **The map** (WORLD → Map, `scripts/ui/panels/map_panel.gd`): as large as the screen allows; **dragged to look about, − and + to come closer** (up to 8×); **layers on and off: Ground, Water, Settlements (fires and their names, ruins), Unexplored, Events**; a tap on a place takes the camera there and puts the map away. Opens in ~40 ms on the box of 64.
- **Deviations:** the map's fog is the dimming of the unknown here; the three layers of knowledge (player-seen, civ-explored, civ-mapped) are M13.4. No pinch to zoom on the map yet (− and +); the minimap shows the box at one size (no zoom). Important places are the ruins for now (discoveries come later).
- **Verified:** `test_minimap` (6): **`test_minimap_tap_to_world`** (the point and the place agree; the camera goes there), `test_a_drag_scrubs`, `test_the_map_image` (a pixel a tile; water blue; the unknown dimmed and brighter once explored; a changed chunk drawn again), `test_marks_and_folding` (the fire, the ruin, the one selected, the camera's outline; folded and remembered), `test_the_map` (WORLD → Map; layers; zoom; a drag is not a tap; a tap goes there and puts it away), `test_the_map_opens_quickly` (< 100 ms; 38 ms). Changed: `test_hamburger_menu` (Map is there now). **Full suite (alone): 1265 passed, 0 failed** (the band's day 0.713 ms). Desktop screenshots, Mobile and Compatibility (`C:/tmp/m133/`): the minimap in its corner, the map, zoomed without the unexplored layer.

### M13.4 Fog of knowledge & exploration — ✅ DONE (2026-10-04)
- [x] **Three layers of knowledge** (`scripts/world/fog_of_knowledge.gd`, `FogOfKnowledge`; bible §8.7), a byte a tile in chunks of the knowledge's own, saved with it (not on the land's chunks: knowing a place does not change it — kept on them, an untouched world saved seven changed chunks): **seen by the player** — what the camera shows from up close (within 30 tiles; WorldView notes it twice a second); **explored by the world** — where anyone has been **and what they saw from there** (6 tiles all round, once a game hour, everyone out of doors; the land about home known from the start); **mapped** — on their maps: none yet (cartography, M18). The minimap and the map show the world's knowledge (M13.3 used the explorers' squares: now this).
- [x] **The fog drawn:** land nobody knows — neither the player nor anyone in the world — is **dimmed and greyed, never black** (`assets/shaders/fog.gdshaderinc`, in the ground's, the props' and the water's shaders: a texel a tile, `fog_strength` 0.6); drawn again per chunk as knowledge grows. From afar the box is a diorama with its known heart in colour; a camera that comes close lifts the fog where it looks.
- [x] **Explorers:** **the adventurous go further** (up to 12 tiles beyond the usual 20, by how adventurous they are) — **setting out in the morning**, to be back by dark; a child does not tag along to a parent far from home. **The first to see a region finds it** ("Ama has found the eastern hills"). **The first to stand at a wall comes to the Edge** ("Ama has come to the Edge: the land ends at a wall nobody can pass") — **the first thing the world knows of the box** (the settlement knows `the_edge`; box research builds on it later).
- [x] **Regions** (`scripts/world/regions.gd`): worked out from the land — blocks of 8 × 8 tiles sorted into **water, hills and lowland** by their water, rock and height, joined with their like (crumbs of fewer than three blocks join a neighbour) — and **named by what they are and where they lie**: "the river", "the valley", "the eastern hills", "the north-western lowlands" (a name taken twice: "… beyond"). **WORLD → Regions** (once one is found): each with how much of it is explored; a tap takes the camera there. Names from the people's own words come with M17.
- **Caught while building:** the knowledge first kept on the land's chunk flags made an untouched world save seven chunks (`test_world_persistence`). The longer explorations shifted the band's day and showed two old faults: **a child who had just fallen asleep was taught** (a talk now ends when the other falls asleep), **and that lesson wiped the nap from the day's log** (a moment between two people no longer undoes what someone is doing) — so a child seemed never to go to bed (`test_day_log`). A soak's box unfolded and reported "regions found 9 of 7": found regions are now worked out from the land explored.
- **Deviations:** what the world knows does not fade yet (oral knowledge fading, written maps lasting: with cartography, M18). Regions are not saved: they are worked out again from the land when it is opened, and **which are found is worked out from the land explored** (so one already known is never found twice, even after the box unfolds). The minimap's dots for people are not dimmed by the fog.
- **Verified:** `test_fog_of_knowledge` (7): **`test_fog_layers`** (home known; far land none of the three; someone goes there: explored, as far as they see — not seen by the player; the player looks close up: seen, once; nothing mapped; saved and opened), `test_the_regions_of_the_box` (several, each its own name, the river among them, no crumbs, every tile in one, named by where they lie), `test_explorers_find_regions` (in words), `test_the_edge` (the settlement knows the Edge; in words; once), `test_the_adventurous_go_further`, `test_the_fog_is_drawn` (home known, far in the fog, the strength set; seen: out of the fog), `test_after_the_box_unfolds` (every region found is one of the box as it is; nothing known found again). Changed: `test_minimap` (the map reads the world's knowledge). The soak says how many regions were found and whether the Edge was reached. **Full suite (alone): 1272 passed, 0 failed** (the band's day 0.717 ms; 622 decisions — the longer explorations change the band's day). **30-year soaks, all PASSED** (before the last fixes): every world reached the Edge and found its regions (4242: 4 of 4; 99: 5 of 5); **seed 12345 unfolded by itself to 96 × 96** (the first time a box grew in a soak) and founded three camps. Desktop screenshots, Mobile and Compatibility (`C:/tmp/m134/`): the box with its known heart in colour, the edge of the known, the Regions page.

### M13 — Completion summary
All five sub-phases are done (M13.1–M13.5) — **before the VS gate, at the owner's word (VS.1–VS.4 done, VS.5 skipped for now, VS.6 open)**: the world streams what the camera sees and builds it off the main thread, **the box unfolds** as the civilization presses against its walls (and a new world can begin larger), **a minimap and a map** show it from above, **knowledge has three layers** and land nobody knows is dimmed, explorers find regions and the Edge, and anything can be found. The plan's tests: `test_chunk_evict_regenerate_identical`, `test_streaming_loads_visible`, `test_unfold_extends_bounds_and_pathing`, `test_minimap_tap_to_world`, `test_fog_layers`, `test_camera_never_lost` — all in. **Exit criteria** (*the world can become significantly larger without breaking the camera or performance*): a box of 256 streams at 0.3–0.45 ms a frame on desktop (2.4 ms at worst); boxes of 96 and 128 unfold and run in tests and a 30-year soak (seed 12345 unfolded by itself). **Not yet measured on the phone** (streaming hitch < 8 ms a chunk; memory at 256 with two settlements): the phone has the M12 build. Between M13's steps, M12 was finished (crossings bank to bank; camp sites judged by their own water).

### M13 — Tests & checks
**Automated:** `test_chunk_evict_regenerate_identical`, `test_streaming_loads_visible`, `test_unfold_extends_bounds_and_pathing`, `test_minimap_tap_to_world`, `test_fog_layers`, `test_camera_never_lost` (random pans/zooms/unfolds keep pivot valid).
**Performance:** 256×256 world with 2 settlements: memory within budget; streaming hitch < 8 ms per chunk on device.
**Exit criteria:** *The world can become significantly larger without breaking the camera or performance.*

## M14 — HAMBURGER MENU + INFORMATION SYSTEM — ✅ DONE (2026-10-04)

**Goal:** The player understands the state of the world without debug tools. (P:M14, S§8, B§26.5)
**Depends on:** M13.

- [x] Full menu structure (B§26.5) with progressive reveal (hidden until system active).
- [x] Panels: WORLD (Map, World Overview, Regions, Weather, Environment, Resources) · PEOPLE (Population, Individuals with search/sort/filter, Families, Occupations, Relationships, Important People) · CIVILIZATION (Settlements, Buildings, Economy, Agriculture; Technology/Culture/Government/Beliefs/Science stubs appear with their milestones) · HISTORY (Timeline, Events, Discoveries, Disasters, Wars (later), Eras (later)) · PLAYER (Interactions, Statistics, Discoveries, Influence, Box Knowledge (later)) · SETTINGS (Audio, Haptics, Motion, Graphics, Simulation Speed, Accessibility, Notifications, Save, Advanced/seed copy, Debug-if-flag).
- [x] Reusable widgets: list with virtualization, detail card, section header, chip, "Locate" button, empty state text ("Nothing recorded yet.").
- [x] Orientation-responsive layouts (side panel in landscape, full-height sheet in portrait); menu never fully covers the world in landscape.
- [x] Seed display & **Copy seed** (`DisplayServer.clipboard_set`).
**Tests:** `test_menu_visibility_rules`, UI smoke test scene that opens every panel with a large fixture world (no errors, < 100 ms open).
**Exit criteria:** *The player can understand the state of the world without developer tools* — testers answer 5 questions ("How many people? Who's the oldest? What happened last year? Which settlement has most food? What's the weather?") in < 2 minutes.

### M14 — What was built
(Before the VS gate, at the owner's word: VS.1–VS.4 done, VS.5 skipped for now, VS.6 open.)
- [x] **The menu's tree** (`scripts/ui/menu/menu_pages.gd`, `MenuPages.sections`; bible §26.5): **WORLD** (Locate, Map, World Overview, Regions, Weather, Environment, Resources) · **PEOPLE** (Population, Individuals, Families, Occupations, Relationships, Important People) · **CIVILIZATION** (Settlements, Buildings, Economy, Agriculture, Government, Beliefs) · **HISTORY** (Timeline, Major Events, Discoveries, Disasters, Important People, Firsts) · **PLAYER** (Interaction History, Statistics — moved here, as the bible has it — What You Have Seen) · **SETTINGS** (Audio, Haptics, Motion, Graphics, Simulation Speed, Accessibility, Notifications, Save, Advanced, Debug). **Each entry is shown once what it tells of exists — hidden, never greyed:** Economy at the first load carried or a second settlement, Agriculture at the first field, Government once someone leads, Beliefs at the first shared memory or myth, Discoveries at the first found, Disasters at the first, Regions once one is found, Occupations once anyone works, Motion while motion is on, Debug with the debug flag; Technology, Culture, Science, Wars, Eras, Influence, Reputation, Box Knowledge and Achievements wait for their milestones. **A tap on a section's heading folds it away** (and back).
- [x] **The pages:** *World Overview* — the date, how many people in how many settlements, the box (and how often it has unfolded), the weather now, the trees and forest, regions found, whether the Edge has been reached, the seed and **Copy seed** (`DisplayServer.clipboard_set`, a notice says so); *Environment* — season, weather, rain, forest, grass, a dry spell; *Resources* — each settlement's stores and days of food, what stands and lies in the world; *Population* — how many, children/grown/elders, men/women, the age on average, born and died this year, each settlement's, **the oldest and the youngest** (a tap goes to them); *Individuals* — **a search, an order (name, age, work) and a filter (all, children, grown, elders)**; *Occupations* — each work and who does it; *Settlements* — a card each, the largest first: tier and people, who leads, **food for how many days (and how much in store)**, wood/stone/tools, what it is known for, when founded, **Locate**; *Buildings* — what stands in each and what is going up (how far along); *Economy* — loads carried, each settlement's specialty and what it sends and gets; *Agriculture* — fields by stage, farmers, harvests, a dry spell; *Government* — who leads where; *Beliefs* — the shared memories and myths in words; *Major Events* and *Disasters* — the timeline through that filter; *Discoveries* — regions found, the Edge, what was worked out (a tap goes there); *What You Have Seen* — how much of the box the player has seen close up; *Graphics* — quality (Auto, Low, Medium, High) and **frames a second at most (30, 60 — it acts now: `Engine.max_fps`)**; *Simulation Speed*; *Accessibility* — reduced motion, turning with two fingers; *Notifications* — **notices on or off (it acts now)**; *Advanced* — the seed, Copy seed, the world, the version; *Debug* — the overlay.
- [x] **Building blocks** (`MainMenu`): heading, line, entry, a chip row (one chosen), **a detail card with Locate**, toggles, the search box, the empty state (**"Nothing recorded yet."**); long lists are the timeline's virtual list (M11.3). **On its side the menu is a panel down the side, full height, at most 45 % of the width** (`MainMenu.panel_width`); held upright as tall as what it lists.
- **Deviations:** settings that nothing acts on yet are not shown (text size, UI scale, high contrast, phone notifications — they come with the systems that act on them); no Technology, Culture or Science pages (their milestones). The Individuals list is drawn as buttons (a world of a few dozen; the timeline's virtual list is there for longer ones). The menu's pages are drawn anew when opened (cheap: the slowest, Locate, ~22 ms with 58 people in a box of 128).
- **Caught while building:** the menu's old page lingered in its list while being freed, so the new page's names met it and were changed — and the fold-a-section button was pressed twice (it is taken out of the list at once now).
- **Verified:** `test_menu_m14` (5): **`test_menu_visibility_rules`** (what is always there; what waits — Economy, Agriculture, Government, Beliefs, Disasters, Discoveries, Debug — and comes with what it tells of; motion stays on hold; a section folds and opens), **`test_every_page_opens_on_a_large_world`** (the plan's smoke test: all 32 pages on a box of 128 with 58 people and many events, each in < 100 ms, each showing something), **`test_the_five_questions`** (the exit criterion's: how many people, who is the oldest, what happened, which settlement has the most food, what the weather is), `test_individuals_searched_ordered_filtered`, `test_settings_that_act` (notices off and on, frames a second, the speed from the menu, the seed, the side panel's width). Changed: `test_hamburger_menu` and `test_family_ui` (CIVILIZATION is there now). **Full suite (alone): 1282 passed, 0 failed** (the band's day 0.702 ms). Desktop screenshots (`C:/tmp/m14/`): the menu, World Overview, Population, Individuals with its search and chips, Settlements, Graphics; on its side.
- **Exit criteria** (*testers answer five questions in < 2 minutes*): each answer is one or two taps from the ☰ (Overview: people, weather; Population or Individuals by age: the oldest; Timeline or Major Events: what happened; Settlements: food); **the timed test with players is not done** (with VS.6).

## M15 — STATISTICS — ✅ DONE (2026-10-04)

**Goal:** Turn the simulation into something players enjoy studying. (P:M15, S§9, B§27.1)
**Depends on:** M14.

- [x] `StatsRecorder` full: all categories (B§27.1) derived from sim data; multi-resolution time series (hourly for recent days, daily for years, yearly for centuries) with bounded storage; saved.
- [x] `scripts/ui/widgets/chart.gd`: sparkline, line chart (pan/zoom time), stacked bars (age distribution), colourblind-safe palette + shape markers.
- [x] Panels: Population · Economy · Society · Environment · Player (B§27.3 counters).
- [x] **Progressive reveal:** stat appears after the underlying system produces data (e.g., inequality only after multiple households with differing wealth).
- [x] Derived "insight" lines ("Food production fell 30% after the drought of Year 12.") using events + series.
**Tests:** `test_stats_derived_not_static` (changing sim data changes stats), `test_series_downsampling`, `test_stats_persistence`.
**Exit criteria:** Testers voluntarily open statistics twice in a session and find something they didn't know.

### M15 — What was built
(Before the VS gate, at the owner's word: VS.1–VS.4 done, VS.5 skipped for now, VS.6 open.)
- [x] **The numbers, worked out from the world** (`scripts/simulation/stats_sampler.gd`, `StatsSampler.sample`; `WorldSession.sample_stats` hands over to it): 38 of them — *population*: people, born, died, average age, children/adults/elders, settlements, moves away, health, fed, unwell; *economy*: food in store and how many days it lasts, water, wood, stone, tools, brought in, trade trips, buildings, fields; *society*: mood, fear, fondness (the average affinity between those who know each other), friendships, rivalries; *environment*: weather (temperature), rain, forest, grass, trees, wild animals, soil (the average fertility, every fourth tile); *the player*: touches, who remember the player, the share who believe in a god, or in nature. Nothing is counted by hand: each is read off the people, the stores, the events, the land when the hour comes.
- [x] **Kept at three resolutions** (`StatsRecorder`): every hour (the recent 40 days, as before), **every day — the day's average — for a hundred years, every year — the year's average — for two thousand**; each a ring of its own, so it stays bounded however long the world lives. The day and the year being gathered are saved too, so a world saved part way through a day carries on as if never saved; **a world saved before M15 reads as before** (its hours; the numbers not kept then are blank; days and years begin).
- [x] **The chart** (`scripts/ui/widgets/chart.gd`): lines over time — **dragged to look back, zoomed (+ and −, the mouse wheel)**, the scale fitting what is in view, the first and last dates below; or **stacked bars**. Colours anyone can tell apart (Okabe–Ito) and **every line its own mark** (circle, square, triangle, diamond). New samples keep the window where it was (and one showing now keeps showing now).
- [x] **The panel** (`StatsPanel`, rebuilt): tabs **Population · Economy · Society · Environment · You** (the player's: what was done and what the world makes of it — the history card's counters — then the player's numbers). Each number as it is now, with its line; **a tap opens it**: its value, a chart by **Hours, Days, Years** (each once there are two to draw), and what the numbers say about it. On Population, **the ages — women and men, by ten years — as stacked bars**. It is built anew only when what it shows changes (a new tab, a number revealed, an insight); otherwise only the numbers move.
- [x] **Shown once there is something** (`StatsCatalog`): people, average age, adults, settlements, health, fed, food, its days, water, mood, the weather, forest, grass, trees and soil from the start; every other number once it has been other than nothing (born, died, rivalries, trade trips, rain, the player's…); a tab with nothing in it is not there (the player's always is).
- [x] **What the numbers say** (`scripts/simulation/insights.gd`): for each drought, flood, storm, cold snap, heat wave, shortage, failed harvest, new settlement, unfolding of the box and touch of the player, the numbers it may have changed are compared — the daily averages of the six days before and after (three at the least each side) — and **a change of a quarter or more is said: "Food in store fell 50% after the drought in year 1."** The largest first, three on a tab, five on a number; one a year for each kind and number.
- **Deviations:** no inequality yet (no households with differing wealth: it comes with them, by the same rule). "Brought in" is the settlements' produced counters together. Interventions, births, deaths and moves are counts so far (the chart shows them rising); "per year" waits for a reason. On its side the panel scrolls to the chart (the tabs make way while a number is open).
- **Verified:** `test_statistics_m15` (7): **`test_stats_derived_not_static`** (a death and wood added change the numbers; every series has a value; the ages add up), **`test_series_downsampling`** (days as the averages of their hours, dated by their first; years of days; the hours bounded, the oldest let go), **`test_stats_persistence`** (saved part way through a day and carried on = never saved; a world from before M15; with the world through var_to_bytes), `test_insights` (the drought halves the food: said; the unchanged water is not; too few days: nothing), `test_reveal` (born appears once someone is; every series has a tab, a name, a way to be written), `test_chart` (zoom, pan within bounds, the scale, following now, bars), `test_panel_tabs_and_detail` (tabs, ages = the living, a tap opens the chart, Days, a new sample doesn't rebuild or unzoom, back, the player's tab). Changed: `test_statistics_panel` (numbers on their tabs). **Full suite (alone): 1289 passed, 0 failed.** Caught by it: the empty day and year rings made a pristine world's save too big — empty rings are not saved now. Desktop screenshots (`C:/tmp/m15/`): Population with the ages, Economy, food by the hour and by the day with its insight, You; upright and on its side (the forty days drawn there are made up around the world's own numbers, a drought among them).
- **Exit criteria** (*testers open statistics twice in a session and find something they didn't know*): **needs players** (with VS.6).

## M16 — TECHNOLOGY — ✅ DONE (2026-10-04)

**Goal:** Long-term progression; civilization visibly changes as knowledge increases. (P:M16, S§27, B§18)
**Depends on:** M15. **Split into 3 sub-phases.**

### M16.1 Knowledge model — ✅ DONE (2026-10-04)
- [x] `scripts/civilization/knowledge.gd`: domain points per person and per settlement (B§18.1); accrual from work/curiosity/teaching/failure/player interventions; oral knowledge loss on death without students; teaching action.

**What was built:** `Knowledge` (`session.learning`; the name `knowledge` was taken by the fog of knowledge, M13.4). **Ten domains** — the land, making things, growing food, building, healing, counting, the sky, keeping records, living together, the unexplained (Anomaly) — **held by people** (`PersonData.knowledge["domains"]`, saved with them: no format change), 0 … 100 each, coming slower as 100 is neared. **A settlement knows what its most knowing member knows and a fifth of what each of the others does** (`Knowledge.together`), or what its records keep, the more. **Learned, once a game day:** from the day's work (a farmer growing food, a builder building, a trader counting and living together, an elder the old stories — scaled by skill and wits), from curiosity (the land, the sky — and elders, the sky), from living together (everyone grown), **children by watching** the most knowing of their household; **from nursing** the ill of one's household (healing); **from what goes wrong** — a failed or frozen crop or a poor harvest teaches the farmers, a shortage the land, a flood and storm damage building, spoiled food making things; **from the player's doing** — every memory of it feeds the unexplained, by how much it mattered; something the player moved, come upon, making things (a first flint); **taught** — the TEACH act (M10) now passes on some of what the teacher knows best. **When someone dies, what only they knew goes with them**: if a settlement loses a third or more of a domain it knew at least 12 points of, it is told ("When Mara died, much of what was known of healing went with them"); what they had taught, the student keeps. **Writing (M16.2) keeps half** in the settlement's records (saved). Debug overlay: "knowledge" line.
- **Verified:** `test_knowledge` (4): `test_the_day_teaches` (a year of farming teaches growing food; everyone living together; a child by watching; never past 100, slower near it), `test_a_settlement_knows_what_its_most_knowing_member_knows` (the share of the others; a lesson; clean reading of what was saved), **`test_knowledge_loss_oral`** (the only healer dies: told, and nobody knows it now; the teacher of a student who knows nearly as much: nothing told; written down: half kept), `test_what_goes_wrong_and_the_player_teach` (a failed crop teaches the farmers only; a touch feeds the unexplained; saved with the person). **Full suite (alone): 1298 passed, 0 failed.**

### M16.2 Technology system — ✅ DONE (2026-10-04)
- [x] `data/technologies/*.tres` (`TechnologyDef`, B§18.2) — Fire through Mathematics/Engineering first (Electricity/Industrialization/Computing/Advanced Science defined but gated by later content).
- [x] `technology_system.gd` (daily): evaluate discovery opportunities from conditions → roll → pick inventor (skill/curiosity/intelligence weighted) → `technology_discovered` + event + notification ("Someone has discovered a new use for copper.") + significance to inventor.
- [x] Unlocks: buildings, occupations, actions, interpretations (PHYSICS/EXPERIMENT later), vocabulary concepts, visuals.
- [x] Tech diffusion between settlements via trade/migration.

**What was built:** `TechnologyDef` (`scripts/civilization/technology_def.gd`: id, domain, prerequisites, required resources, knowledge threshold, least people, a trade someone must follow, buildings that must stand, a chance a day, unlocks, era weight; and `known_from_start`, `own_rule`, `implemented`, `order`) and `TechnologyLibrary` (checks the chain: every prerequisite defined, nothing its own ancestor). **25 technologies** in `data/technologies/`, in the bible's order: **known from the start** — fire, foraging, planting (the band arrives knowing them: the game has farmed since M7); **toolmaking** (worked out by its own rule since M12.4, listed with the rest); **discovered** — pottery, weaving, herb lore (medicine), lamps, writing, sky lore (astronomy), counting (mathematics), road building (engineering); **defined but not yet possible** (`implemented = false`, resting on what the world does not have: copper, tin, iron, coal, herds) — herding, copper, bronze, iron, natural philosophy, the scientific method, printing, mechanics, industry, electricity, chemistry, computing, advanced science. **`TechnologySystem`** (`session.technology`), once a game day, for every settlement and every technology it does not know: **what keeps it from it** (`missing`: what must be known first; what must be at hand — clay where there is water near the fire, fibre and herbs where bushes grow (herbs: and someone forages), fat from what is hunted or fished, stones (or rocks near the fire), anything else what it has had; enough people; the trade; the buildings; enough known of the domain); when nothing does, **a chance a day** (more the more is known beyond what it takes, up to three times); **the inventor** — drawn among the grown, weighed by what they know of it, their curiosity and wits (twice for the trade it takes) — **is told of** ("Teo has worked out how to fire clay into pots": the M12.4 `knowledge_learned` event, a notice, and the inventor's significance through it). Every roll is a hash of the world's numbers. **What one settlement knows travels**: a new settlement takes along all that its founders' settlement knew; **each load carried** may bring how something is done (6 %), if the receiving settlement has what it takes ("… has brought back from Ama's camp how to fire clay into pots": `knowledge_spread`). **Writing keeps records** (M16.1: half of what is known survives whoever dies). Debug overlay: "technology" line (known, found, spread, the age, what is close).
- **Deviations:** the band knows planting from the start (making it a discovery would undo M7–M12's food); the existing toolmaking rule (M12.4) is kept and listed as `own_rule`; unlocks of occupations, interpretations and vocabulary are listed in the data for M17+ (nothing reads them yet); a discovery's notification is the existing notice of its event.
- **Verified:** `test_technology` (4): `test_the_chain_is_whole`, **`test_tech_requires_conditions`** (a technology needing copper is never worked out in a thousand years of days with everything else known; nothing before what it rests on; clay by the river), **`test_tech_inventor_attribution`** (the one who knows most, mostly; the event names them; once only), **`test_tech_diffusion`** (founded: taken along; loads: sooner or later, and only what can be used).

### M16.3 Visible change — ✅ DONE (2026-10-04)
- [x] Every implemented tech changes something visible (B§18.3): tools in hands, new building variants, lamps at night, pottery props, clothing colours, new sounds.
- [x] CIVILIZATION → Technology panel: known techs as a timeline + "what they're close to" hints (vague: "Potters are experimenting with fire and clay.").
- [x] Civilization phase evaluator (B§22.1) → era events ("The Age of Copper").

**What was built:** four new buildings, each with its shape, built once by a settlement of six or more that knows what it is for (`SettlementPlanner.LANDMARKS`; PropData kinds appended: KILN, HERB_RACK, RECORD_STONE, STONE_CIRCLE): **a kiln** (pottery — a clay dome, its mouth glowing, pots cooling beside it), **a herb rack** (herb lore — bundles drying on a pole), **a record stone** (writing — a slab with rows of marks; **tallies** cut on its back once they count), **a stone circle** (sky lore). **Pots hold more**: a storehouse holds half as much again with pottery, a quarter more again with counting. **Dyed cloth**: the people of a settlement that weaves wear madder, indigo, saffron, woad (`PersonMeshLibrary.CLOTH_DYED`). **Lamps by the doors at night** — a flame and a warm pool of light before every hut of a settlement that knows lamps (`LampsView`). **Herb lore tends the ill**: they recover half as fast again. **Road building paves the paths** near the fire with stones (a new terrain, PAVED: grey, walked a little more gladly than a path, never grown over). **CIVILIZATION → Technology** (shown once anything beyond the beginning is known or close): the age now; what is known — "Fire, Foraging, Planting: known from the beginning", "Year 4 · Pottery — worked out by Teo", "… — brought from Ama's camp by …"; and **what they are close to**, vaguely ("Someone keeps shaping river clay and leaving it by the fire."). **The ages** (`CivilizationPhase`, bible §22.1, derived): primitive survival → **settling** (homes and a storehouse) → **farming** (the fields bring in half the food) → **villages** (a village with a workshop, kiln or herb rack, or a toolmaker or trader) → **towns** (a town that writes) → **peoples** (two settlements, trade between them) → science, industry, lights (resting on what is not yet possible); **the first time each is reached, history says so** ("The age of farming has begun: the fields feed more than the wild"); the age now may fall back, the ages reached stay (saved).
- **Deviations:** no new sounds yet (the kiln and the rest knock and hum like the buildings they are); tools in hands were M12.4's; the ages of the box and the outside wait for the research lines.
- **Verified:** `test_tech_visible` (8): **`test_every_implemented_technology_changes_something_visible`** (each has a building or a visual; each building is built only once it is known, and has a shape), `test_what_is_known_is_built`, `test_pots_and_counting_hold_more`, `test_tended_and_dyed`, `test_lamps_burn_at_night`, `test_roads_are_paved`, **`test_phase_evaluator`** (primitive → settling → farming; falls back; each age told once), `test_the_technology_page`. Changed: `test_interaction_manager`, `test_construction` (the new kinds), `test_technology` (the retuned thresholds). **Full suite (alone): 1309 passed, 1 failed** — `test_version_26_save_loads` expected an old world to know nothing; it knows the beginning now (changed, run again: 7/7). **Tuning soaks** (`soak_m7`, 40 years, seed 12345; the soak now advances knowledge and technology and lists what was worked out): first tuning — pottery, weaving and lamps all in years 3–4, writing never (keeping records held only by elders, lost five times): too quick and stuck. Retuned (thresholds up, chances down; elders and traders keep records; everyone hears the old stories; rocks near the fire count as stones): **pottery and weaving year 8, lamps 10, writing 11, herb lore 19, sky lore 33, counting 36** (others' camps worked out herb lore themselves; sky lore travelled once by trade); two losses of the old stories in 40 years; PASSED. Road building, never in 40 years (it waits on counting and many builders). The age stayed **settling**: the fields never brought in half the food on this seed. Desktop screenshots (`C:/tmp/m16/`): the kiln, record stone, herb rack and stone circle by day, dyed cloth, a paved road; the lamps by the doors at night. **The plan's soak — 300 years on 5 seeds — not run** (multi-hour: the owner's word first).
**Exit criteria** (*civilization visibly changes as knowledge increases*): over 40 years one settlement goes from fire and foraging to kilns, dyed cloth, lamps at night, a record stone, a stone circle and tallies — each worked out by someone history remembers. To be judged by eye on the phone.
**Tests:** `test_tech_requires_conditions` (no discovery without resources even after 1,000 years), `test_tech_inventor_attribution`, `test_knowledge_loss_oral`, `test_tech_diffusion`, `test_phase_evaluator`.
**Soak:** 300 game years on 5 seeds: tech order varies with conditions; no tech appears without prerequisites.
**Exit criteria:** *Civilization visibly changes as knowledge increases.*

## M17 — CULTURE AND BELIEF (+ LANGUAGE) — ✅ DONE (2026-10-04)

**Goal:** Civilizations become culturally distinct; player interventions can become culturally significant. (P:M17, S§29–31, B§19)
**Depends on:** M16. **Split into 3 sub-phases.**

### M17.1 Culture profile & traditions — ✅ DONE (2026-10-04)
- [x] `culture_system.gd`: profile per settlement (B§19.1) derived yearly; traditions from repeated emotional shared events (B§19.2–19.3); rituals as scheduled social activities; holidays in calendar; festivals as world events ("The harvest festival starts at dusk.").
- [x] Architecture style & clothing palette variants per culture (visual parameters).

**What was built:** `CultureSystem` (`session.cultures`; `session.culture` stays the M11 cultural memory). **A profile** per settlement: tradition ↔ innovation (its people's curiosity, what it has worked out), self ↔ community (generosity and company), skepticism ↔ piety (the share of what they take for someone's doing), what it makes of the Presence (the interpretation most hold, and how many), its myths, its traditions, its look. **Traditions from what is lived again and again, felt and taken alike**: a cultural memory held by a third of a settlement, lived on five days over two seasons, becomes one — rain from a clear sky **the rain dance** (danced when a drought begins), the player's touches **the Blessing of the Young**, things moved through the air **the laying of offering stones**, a shaken tree **the singing to the trees**, a sourceless wind **the wind festival**, poured water **the gift of water**, a flood lived through **the Day of High Water**, a storm **the storm vigil**; and firsts — the first field **the harvest festival** (the first day of autumn), the first bridge **the bridge festival** (its day). **What began with the player's doing says so** — "Ama's camp has begun to keep the rain dance — it began with something nobody could explain" — and keeps the act it goes back to (the one most of them remember). **Holidays**: each keeps its day in the year; **at dusk on it everyone goes to the fire to dance** (a new activity, `celebrate`, open only while a festival is on, three hours) — "The harvest festival begins at dusk in Ama's camp" (an event, a notice). A tradition whose root nobody remembers for six years fades (told). A settlement founded from another keeps its traditions. **Its look**: homes thatched the band's way, with reeds, or with hides (hut variants, chosen per settlement and kept); its own colours of cloth (a palette shift per settlement). Debug overlay: "cultures" line.
- **Deviations:** the profile is worked out daily (cheap; it feeds belief, M17.2); music, art motifs, taboos: later; no temple calendar beyond the festivals.
- **Verified:** `test_culture` (5): `test_the_profile` (values in range; all pious → piety 1, the Presence a god; other settlements look otherwise; three roofs), **`test_tradition_from_repetition`** (twice is not a tradition; five days over two seasons is: the rain dance, the player's, act 42, told with its line; once; a drought begins → at dusk the dance, `celebrate` to the fire; saved), `test_firsts_become_festivals_kept_each_year`, `test_a_tradition_fades_when_its_root_is_forgotten`, `test_homes_are_roofed_their_settlements_way`. Changed: `test_behavior` (the new activity). **Full suite (alone): 1315 passed, 0 failed.**

### M17.2 Belief & religion — ✅ DONE (2026-10-04)
- [x] `belief.gd` aggregation: interpretation distribution per settlement feeds culture priors (closing the loop with M5 interpretation).
- [x] `myth_system.gd` full: myths with attributes, sacred places (tile flag SACRED), shrines/temples via planner, religious founders (important people), schisms & spread.
- [x] CIVILIZATION → Beliefs, Culture panels.

**What was built:** **What a settlement believes, all told**, is worked out daily with its profile (the share of its people's belief in each interpretation, `CultureSystem.belief_share`) and **leans how each of its people takes what happens** (`Interpretation`: a new term beside the culture prior, the leader's belief and the cultural memory — the loop with M5 closed; no separate `belief.gd`). **`MythSystem`** (`session.faith`; the myths themselves stay in CulturalMemory, M11.2): when a myth forms, **the places where it showed itself are sacred** (`ChunkData.FLAG_SACRED`); **someone speaks for it** — of its settlement, whoever holds that agent's doing most, weighted by how much they matter — its **founder** ("Teo has begun to speak for the Rainbringer": a significant event, so history keeps them); **a shrine** (a new building and shape: a roofed stone, a small flame, offerings) is built **by its sacred place** once four or more believe (`SettlementPlanner.shrine_site`); **it travels** — founders take their settlement's myths along, a load may carry one ("The tale of the Rainbringer has reached Ama's camp"); **it splits** — when two myths of the same thing (a god's doing, a spirit's) are each held by a quarter of a settlement, history tells of a schism ("Ama's camp is divided over a touch from an unseen hand: some say it is a god's doing, others a spirit's"). **CIVILIZATION → Culture** (new): each settlement — what it values, what happens is to them (and how many hold it), its traditions and when they are kept ("· it began with you"), its roofs, its words. **Beliefs** now lists each settlement's myths with believers, sacred places, who speaks for them, its shrine — then the events as before.
- **Deviations:** no temples (a larger building, M18+), and religion does not yet decline under scientific scrutiny (science is M18).

### M17.3 Language (structured vocabulary) — ✅ DONE (2026-10-04)
- [x] `lexicon.gd`: concept registry (`data/text/concepts.csv`), culture phonology (from M4 name generator), word coining by concept frequency, inheritance, borrowing, drift after separation (B§19.5).
- [x] UI shows words with glosses everywhere names appear (myths, places, eras, the Presence).
- [x] Settlement/region names switch from placeholders to lexicon names (with history record "River Town came to be called Tiravel").

**What was built:** `Lexicon` (`session.lexicon`). **Concepts** (`Lexicon.CONCEPTS`, glossed in `history.csv` as CONCEPT_* and the myths' epithets): the Presence, the unseen touch, rain from a clear sky, things flying through the air, the wind from nowhere, the flood, the Rainbringer and the other myths, their own place, the Edge, the river, the hills, the valley. Each is counted daily by **how much a settlement's people meet it** (the holders of a cultural memory of it; the believers of its myth; everyone, their own place; the Edge once found; the regions found); **at 40 people-days it gets a word**, made in the settlement's sounds (the M4 phonology of its culture), deterministic for the world. **A settlement takes its word for its own place as its name** ("The first camp came to be called Shiashe" — an event). **Inherited**: a settlement founded from another speaks as it did (its words and sounds; its own place it names itself); **borrowed**: a load may bring a word the receivers had met but had no word for; **drift**: once apart ten years, each shared word may change a sound in a year (4 %), each settlement its own way (`Lexicon.kinship`: how alike their words sound, letter by letter). **Glosses**: the myths' names in events and on the Beliefs page ("Skufa (the Rainbringer)"), regions ("Yuwur (the river)" on the Regions page), the Culture page's words. Debug overlay / soak: the words of each settlement.
- **Deviations:** the concept registry is in code with its glosses in `history.csv` (a separate concepts.csv would need registering as a translation); eras keep their English names; region names are the home settlement's words.
- **Verified:** `test_belief` (3): `test_what_a_settlement_holds_leans_how_its_people_take_things`, `test_a_myth_hallows_its_place_and_is_spoken_for` (sacred; founder told and significant; a shrine wanted, by the sacred place), **`test_myth_split_merge`** (one belief: no schism; two: told once; founders take myths along; loads carry one). `test_lexicon` (3): **`test_lexicon_coining_threshold`** (not after three days; in time a word for home, and the settlement goes by it, told; no word for what is not met; glosses; the same world, the same words), **`test_word_inheritance`** (inherited, home their own; borrowed with loads, only what was met), **`test_culture_divergence_after_split`** (one language at the split; a hundred years later less alike, still kin; what each coins apart differs; they lean otherwise; saved). `test_culture` gained **`test_everyone_comes_to_the_festival`** (most of the settlement is at the fire). Changed: `test_construction`, `test_interaction_manager` (the shrine). **Full suite (alone, beside the soak): 1322 passed, 0 failed.** **Soak** (`soak_m7`, 40 years, seed 12345; it now advances cultures, faith and words and lists what came of them; PASSED): the first camp keeps the harvest festival every year (40 times) and the bridge festival; the camps founded from it keep them too; a storm vigil formed in year 5, faded in year 11, formed again in year 38; every settlement came to go by its own word — the first camp became **Miska** (year 2), then **Nouguth**, **Skikoush**, **Lilima**; their words for the valley, the river, the hills, the Edge, the flood are **kin but drifting** (Kufi / Kufe, Skouthi / Skiothi, Chumam / Chumem / Chumom); a word for the Presence in two of them. No myths, founders or shrines in the soak (nobody plays: myths come of the player's doing) — `test_belief` covers them. Desktop screenshots (`C:/tmp/m17/`): the Culture page ("The rain dance — when the land goes dry · it began with you", "In their words: Skufa (the Rainbringer), Thushi (the Edge), Yuwur (the river)"); the evening of the harvest festival.
**Exit criteria** (*two settlements clearly distinguishable by beliefs, names and rituals; at least one tradition traceable to a player action*): names and words — yes (soak); rituals — they share what they took along, and differ by what each lived since (the storm vigil is Miska's alone); beliefs differ by what each believes all told (profile) and its myths (none without a player); **a tradition traceable to the player** — yes (`test_tradition_from_repetition`: the rain dance, and the act it began with). To be judged by eye with a player's world.

**Tests:** `test_tradition_from_repetition`, `test_culture_divergence_after_split` (two settlements separated 100 years differ in priors/lexicon), `test_lexicon_coining_threshold`, `test_word_inheritance`, `test_myth_split_merge`.
**Exit criteria:** Two settlements in one world are clearly distinguishable by beliefs, names and rituals; at least one tradition traceable to a player action.

## M18 — SCIENCE AND THE PLAYER MYSTERY — ✅ DONE (2026-10-04)

**Goal:** The beginning of the deeper mystery: scientists notice patterns. (P:M18, S§28, S§35, B§20, B§25.2)
**Depends on:** M17.

- [x] `anomaly_archive.gd`: anomalies from observed stimuli (oral → written after Writing), witness counts, attributes (time of day, location, type, context).
- [x] `science_system.gd`: scientist occupation (after Natural philosophy), investigation actions (travel, observe, record), **correlation checks** (B§20.2 table: time-of-day clustering using the player's real sessions, world-scale tilt, rain-after-drought responsiveness, watched-area correlation), hypotheses as records with confidence; publications → notifications ("Scientists noticed a strange correlation between rainfall and your interactions.").
- [x] Seeded mysteries activated (`data/mysteries/*.tres`): artifact, ruins, ancient structure, recurring anomaly, missing civilization, impossible material, terrain pattern — each a clue chain with conditions (B§25.2).
- [x] `box_research.gd` stages 1–4 (B§20.3): anomaly noticed → pattern → external force hypothesis → Edge expeditions.
- [x] PHYSICS and EXPERIMENT interpretations unlocked by tech; science vs faith dynamics.
- [x] PLAYER → Box Knowledge panel: what the civilization *thinks* (hypotheses, confidence), never the truth.
**Tests:** `test_anomaly_recorded_only_if_witnessed`, `test_time_of_day_correlation_detects_pattern` (synthetic sessions at 20:00 → hypothesis forms), `test_mystery_chain_progression`, `test_box_research_stage_gates`.
**Exit criteria:** In an accelerated test world, scientists form at least one correct-ish hypothesis about the player; the reveal is partial and intriguing.

### M18 — What was built
(Before the VS gate, at the owner's word: VS.1–VS.5 done, VS.6 open.)
- [x] **The anomaly archive** (`scripts/science/anomaly_archive.gd`, `session.anomaly_archive`): **every doing of the player's that anyone saw** — what it was, where, the world's hour and **the player's own real hour** (the system clock), how many saw it, whether the land was in drought, the act it was, and the settlement nearest it. **Nothing unwitnessed, nothing of nature's.** Before a settlement writes, its anomalies are **oral** — forgotten after twenty years; once it writes, what is still remembered is written down and kept.
- [x] **Scholars and scientists** (`scripts/science/science_system.gd`, `session.science`): **natural philosophy** is discoverable now (the unexplained known to 40, writing and sky lore, twelve people) and brings **the scientist** (an occupation: a scroll in hand; their work — to where something strange happened, to crouch and look, and to write it down; "looks into something strange" in their day). Before that, once a settlement writes, **its most curious person** looks into it now and then. Once a day they run the **correlation checks** (bible §20.2) on what their settlement knows: **the phenomena cluster at an hour of the day** — the player's real schedule ("The strange things happen mostly in the evening"); **rain from a clear sky answers drought** (a responsive agent); **it happens where people are to see it** (the observer looks where it acts); **nothing leaves the Edge** (enclosure, once an expedition has come back). Each is a **hypothesis** with a confidence that grows with the evidence; written up (with writing) it is told, its discoverer named. A publication moves those who can think in such terms towards PHYSICS (science and faith: patterned play feeds the checks, capricious play the myths).
- [x] **PHYSICS and EXPERIMENT**: natural philosophy opens PHYSICS to its settlement's people (their knowledge gate), the scientific method EXPERIMENT (still not possible: later content).
- [x] **The box research, stages 1–4** (in `ScienceSystem`; no separate `box_research.gd`): **an anomaly noticed** (the first recorded) → **a pattern** (the first hypothesis: "{name} proposes that the strange things are connected") → **an external force** (two hypotheses held with confidence: "… a single force outside the world is behind them") → **expeditions to the Edge** (a scientist, once the Edge is found: "… has set out with an expedition to study the Edge"; back twelve days later: "it is smooth, and it bends the eye"). **A stage at a time, never within a year of the last** (bible §20.5: understanding is slow).
- [x] **Seven seeded mysteries** (`data/mysteries/*.tres`, `MysteryDef`; `scripts/science/mystery_system.gd`): placed from the world's seed when it is first opened (old stones the world has; standing stones placed for two of them; a spot; a buried place; a place at the Edge), **dormant until each clue's conditions hold** — someone standing near, on enough days; writing, sky lore, counting known; someone looking into the unexplained; the ground over it turned (tilled, flooded, worn); the Edge found; a stone circle standing — **one clue a day at most**, each told with whoever found it: *strange artifact* (turned up in the earth → no one can say what it is made of → its edges are straighter than any tool could cut), *unexplained ruins*, *ancient structure* (→ aligned with the Edge itself), *recurring anomaly* (water gathering uphill), *missing civilization* (marks on old stones → writing, but not ours → numbers), *impossible material* (at the Edge; the last clue dormant: deep mining), *terrain pattern* (from the stone circle the land lies in squares).
- [x] **PLAYER → Box Knowledge** (shown once anything is recorded): how far their understanding has come, how many strange happenings they remember (and how many are written), **what they think** — each hypothesis with how sure they are ("The strange things happen mostly in the evening — near certain"), **what they have found** — the clues. **Never the truth.**
- **Deviations:** tilts are M8 (on hold), so the world-scale-force correlation waits; the external force comes from two confident hypotheses instead. No "rare natural oddities" are archived yet (the recurring anomaly is a mystery's clue chain). The scientific method stays not yet possible.
- **Verified:** `test_science` (5): **`test_anomaly_recorded_only_if_witnessed`** (unseen: no; nature's: no; seen: yes, oral; a touch through the game; forgotten after twenty years unless written; written down and kept), **`test_time_of_day_correlation_detects_pattern`** (synthetic sessions at 20:00 → "mostly in the evening", the discoverer named, on the Box Knowledge page; at every hour: nothing; rain in droughts: answered), **`test_mystery_chain_progression`** (placed the same for the same world; dormant until visited; one clue a day; writing; a scholar; no more), **`test_box_research_stage_gates`** (each stage only when its gate holds, never within a year of the last; the expedition goes and comes back), `test_natural_philosophy_makes_scientists` (PHYSICS opens; a scientist's work goes to look). Changed: `test_world_persistence` (two standing stones of the mysteries in a new world; its save a little larger), `test_occupations` (the scientist), `test_day_log` (their day). **Full suite (alone, beside the soak): 1325 passed, 2 failed** — `test_box_research_stage_gates` (edited while the suite ran; 5/5 alone) and the scientist's day-log words (added; 1/1). **Soak** (`soak_m7`, 40 years, seed 12345, nobody playing; it now advances the archive, science and mysteries; PASSED): no anomalies without a player, so no science; two mysteries gave their first clue — the impossible material once the Edge was found (year 2), the land lying in squares once a stone circle stood (year 21); those that want someone to stand by the old stones never did in 40 years (the stones lie 10+ tiles from the fire, off the paths) — and this run never came to writing. Desktop screenshot (`C:/tmp/m18/`): the Box Knowledge page ("The strange things happen mostly in the evening — near certain").
**Exit criteria** (*in an accelerated world, scientists form at least one correct-ish hypothesis about the player; the reveal partial and intriguing*): with a player's real hours clustered, the time-of-day hypothesis forms and names the player's part of the day (`test_time_of_day_correlation_detects_pattern`); rain answering drought likewise; the Box Knowledge page says what they think and how sure, never more. To be judged with a played world.

## M19 — EMERGENT STORY ENGINE — ✅ DONE (2026-10-04; exit check pending)

**Goal:** Turn simulation events into compelling stories the developers didn't write. (P:M19, S§60, B§21.3)
**Depends on:** M18. **Split into 3 sub-phases.**

### M19.1 Conflict model (prerequisite for the canonical chain)
- [x] Resource/territory disputes between settlements → raids → **war** (abstracted battles, casualties, heroes, survivors) → peace/borders; factions around beliefs/interests; revolution chance (B§17.5). Rare and costly.

### M19.2 Chain mining
- [x] `story_engine.gd`: yearly + on major events, walk causal graph (`causes[]`), score chains (Σ significance, length, novelty, participants' significance), select top chains; avoid repeats.
- [x] Event/era naming ("The Great Drought", "The River War", "The Years of the Leaning World") via templates + lexicon.

### M19.3 Summaries & presentation
- [x] Templates `data/text/history.csv` for chain shapes (cause → consequence → outcome) producing: *"The Great Drought of Year 83 caused the northern migration and eventually contributed to the River War."* / *"…led to the founding of Northwatch."*
- [x] HISTORY → Major Events & Eras show summaries; Player History links interventions to outcomes ("YEAR 31 · saved a settlement from drought").
- [x] "A historian has proposed a new explanation for the Great Flood." — historians reinterpret old events (myth vs record).
**Tests:** `test_chain_detection_on_fixture_graph`, `test_summary_template_filling`, `test_no_duplicate_story`, `test_war_chain_records_causes`.
**Exit criteria:** In 3 soak worlds (300 years), each produces ≥ 3 multi-step summaries a reader finds coherent.

### M19 — What was built
(Before the VS gate, at the owner's word: VS.1–VS.5 done, VS.6 open.)
- [x] **Strife** (`scripts/civilization/conflict_system.gd`, `session.conflicts`; bible §17.5): between every two settlements **a tension**, once a game day — rising when both are hungry and near (30 tiles), when one is hungry and the other not, when they are strangers (their words drifted apart, what they make of the Presence differs), after raids; easing with every trade trip, and with time. High enough: **a dispute** (told, naming the shortage); higher, the hungry side with a **fierce leader**: **a raid** — a third of the other's food carried off, the leader named, the dispute named; **two raids within two years and still bitter: war** (naming the raids). **Battles** now and then (some of the grown on each side fall — less so the brave — and **the bravest who live through it are its heroes**: named in the battle, significant), **the fallen die of the battle** (a new cause of death: "has fallen in the war"); **peace** once four have fallen or two months have passed — **a border** between them, the tension half spent. **Within a settlement: factions** by what its people make of the Presence; when six in ten stand against their leader's belief, a small chance a day of **revolution** — the leader **overthrown** (`Governance.depose`: may not lead again for ten years; "{name} leads Miska now that {other} has been overthrown"). Rare and costly by design.
- [x] **Causes for the new events** (bible §21.2, so the stories can be found): a loss of knowledge names the death; a tradition names the player's act it began with (or the first field, the first bridge, the shared memory); someone speaking for a myth names the myth; a hypothesis names the player's acts its anomalies were; a mystery's clue names the clue before it; a reinterpretation names the story's first event.
- [x] **The story engine** (`scripts/history/story_engine.gd`, `session.stories`): once a game year, and two days after anything momentous, it walks the web of causes back from every event to **the best chain behind it** (most significance, up to eight steps), keeps chains of **three or more**, scores them (what they mattered, length, who was in them — their significance — and how unlike the stories told so far), and **tells the best three a year** that score at least 2.5 — **never one whose end was told, nor one mostly told already** (a new branch from a cause already told of is a new story). **Named** by what heads them — "the Great Drought" (great when it scores high), "the Hungry Summer", "the War of Ama's camp and Miska", "the Founding of Northwatch", "the coming of the Rainbringer"; **put in words** by their shape — "The Great Drought of Year 83 caused a hungry season and eventually led to the founding of Northwatch." / "…caused a failed harvest, which led to…" / "In Year 4, a new bridge led to the bridge festival." (a story headed by something unnamed is told plainly; work begun and finished is said once). Each told is offered as a **notice**. **Historians** (a settlement that writes, and someone looking into the past): now and then one takes up a story at least twenty years old — "Teo, a historian, has proposed a new explanation for the Great Flood".
- [x] **HISTORY → Stories & Eras** (shown once anything is told or an age has begun): the ages, then what history tells, the newest first. **Interaction History**: **"Where it led, in the end"** — the stories the player's doing is in.
- **Deviations:** eras are named by the bible's phases (M16.3), not yet by the lexicon; no story heads are named by the lexicon yet (place names already are, M17.3). Wars do not move people or change cultures yet (borders are recorded, not drawn on the map).
- **Verified:** `test_story` (5): **`test_chain_detection_on_fixture_graph`** (a drought → failed harvest → hunger → leaving → founding found whole, the cause first; noise left out), **`test_summary_template_filling`** (the long, three and named shapes; "the Great Drought" for a high score), **`test_no_duplicate_story`** (told once; one more step is no new story; a new branch is; saved), **`test_war_chain_records_causes`** (two real settlements: dispute ← shortage, raids ← dispute (the leader named), war ← the raids, battles ← the war, the fallen ← a battle, peace ← the war, a border; a story of it), `test_revolution_and_historians`. **Full suite (alone, beside three soaks): 1332 passed, 0 failed** (before the story rules were tightened; `test_story` 6/6 after). **Soak** (`soak_m7`, 40 years, seed 12345; it now advances conflicts and stories and prints every story told; PASSED): the first run told **51 stories** — the same quarrel-and-fight and spoiled-food-and-hunger again and again, "…led to something" where the log had let an event go. Tightened: three a year in all (looking soon after something momentous shares the year's three), **a weighty event in every story** (significance 0.75: a first hungry season, a death, a founding, a war — a quarrel and a fight alone are no story), **the same shape not again within twenty years nor more than three times**, the words kept as told, names keep their capitals. Second run: **6 stories** — "In Year 1, a new bridge led to the bridge festival." / "The Great Storm of Year 4 caused the river rising and eventually led to the seed grain eaten." / "In Year 11, Ditkalou and Guchu falling out brought about Ditkalou and Guchu becoming enemies and eventually led to the founding of Lutsilu's camp." / "In Year 24, food spoiling brought about a hungry season, which led to the stores running empty." Tension between some settlements ran to its height, but no war: no hungry side with a fierce leader in 40 years (rare by design). **Exit criteria** (*3 soak worlds × 300 years, each ≥ 3 multi-step summaries a reader finds coherent*): the 300-year soaks of M19 run overnight (2026-10-04/05) — to be read.


### M19 — follow-up from the 300-year soaks (2026-10-05)
Six 300-year soaks (seeds 12345, 99, 4242; M16–M18 and M19 snapshots, git worktrees) ran overnight; stopped at the owner's word at 09:00 — finished: M16–18 4242 (PASSED), M16–18 99 and M19 4242 (both FAILED one check: dead without a grave); the rest stopped at years 240–295. Every world reached pottery, weaving, herb lore, lamps, writing, sky lore and counting (no road building); **none left the age of settling** (their fields never brought in half their food — a balance question, with the known hunger); every settlement named itself, words drifted; mysteries 3–4 of 7 unfolded; no science (nobody played). Fixed:
- **Graves**: a burial ground fills up over centuries — graves then side by side, further out, then a second burial ground (`test_a_full_burial_ground_does_not_leave_the_dead_unburied`).
- **Stories**: 161 in 300 years, mostly the same hunger ending in nothing ("…led to Shofet getting well") — a story must now **end in an outcome** (a death, a founding, a leaving, war, peace, a leader, a tradition, a myth, a discovery, an age …), the same beginning-and-outcome not again within 30 years (3 times at most), two a year; a death names no story; a battle is "a battle".
- **Wars**: 15 in 300 years killing 51 (seed 4242, M19) — raids only from empty stores and half as often, war after three raids in three years, fewer fall, peace after three.
- **A tradition named once** a settlement (the Day of High Water came twice, from two roots).
- **Cemeteries** (owner's call, 2026-10-05 — replaces the grave-per-person fix above): each settlement has **one cemetery** (`PropData.Kind.CEMETERY`), a fenced 3×3 plot 6–12 tiles from its fire (up to 20 if crowded), opened at its first death; **all its dead are laid there** (the archive keeps everyone laid in it, in order; its headstones grow with them, up to nine shown). Fields and buildings keep off its plot. **Tapping it** (or long press → Read) opens the **cemetery card**: "Cemetery of {place}", how many lie there, and each of the dead — the last laid first, their years and what they died of — each opening their grave card. Mourners go to the cemetery; Locate and the timeline find it. **Older saves**: their single graves are gathered into cemeteries on loading. Verified: `test_graves` (one cemetery for two deaths, the card, saved), `test_the_dead_of_centuries_lie_in_one_cemetery` (300 dead, one cemetery), `test_an_older_saves_graves_are_gathered_into_a_cemetery`.
- **A village starts with 8–11** (owner's call, 2026-10-05): the starting band is 8–11 people in 3–4 households (was 6–8 in 2–3); `test_starting_band`, `test_config`.
- **Owner's playtest notes (2026-10-05)**: children are not drawn into quarrels or fights (the young quarrel, but not to blows; talk with a child never wears on what they are to each other) — `test_children_are_not_drawn_into_quarrels`. Someone going round in circles (hut → bridge → path): work and foraging places with no way to them (across the river, before a crossing) were chosen again and again — now a place a walk to was given up on is not chosen again for a day, or until something (a bridge) is built, and nobody sets out to explore near one; hunting already worked so — `test_nobody_sets_out_again_and_again_for_where_there_is_no_way`. An uprooted tree's trunk holds the wood that was in the tree; woodcutters cut up a fallen trunk near home before felling a standing tree, a load at a time (half the strokes a standing tree takes), until it is gone (not one the player has put somewhere); its card says how much wood is left — `test_an_uprooted_tree_is_cut_up_for_wood`. Likewise the fruit a shaken tree lets go of: foragers pick up fruit lying near home (a piece a unit of berries, as much as they carry) before picking a bush — `test_fallen_fruit_is_picked_up`. (Cones from conifers are still left lying: not food.) The inspect card: no tile numbers; moisture only for what grows (trees, bushes; a field's soil), not for buildings; a building going up shows its **completion** (a percentage), one being mended how far the repair has got. **Builders mend what is worn**: a building is repaired once below 95% (`repair_from`), not only after damage below 70% (weathered buildings were left so) — checked once a day; a home abandoned, or empty past the days before it decays, is left to fall — `test_builders_mend_what_is_worn_and_the_card_says_how_far`. The follow banner ("Resume following …") and the notices under it sit lower — clear of a phone's front camera (120 units down, or below the cutout the phone tells of). Thunder after each flash is heard on a phone (its body now 120–900 Hz, a crack and uneven rolls; three thunders) — `test_lightning_and_thunder`. At most five villagers starred (a sixth is refused, with a word why) — `test_at_most_five_are_starred`. The crickets and the rain are less loopy: a 16 s chorus with near crickets sung over it at random, a 9 s rain whose strength wanders, its loudness and pitch drifting slowly while it falls. **Milestones** (a discovery, a new age, a step in understanding the box): their toast is framed in gold and glows, stays twice as long, is never merged, filtered or dropped — and a **milestone card** tells of it across the dimmed screen until Continue (or Back), one at a time; while the game is in the background they wait for the player's return — `test_a_milestone_is_told_so_it_can_be_read`.
- **Still open**: the exit check (3 worlds × 300 years, ≥ 3 coherent stories each) with these rules — **running** from 2026-10-05 22:49 (five worlds; see the owner's playtest round of 2026-10-05, below M22).
